Gameplay Clips

Record, instant-replay, edit and share gameplay from every Hank's Hits game. Steam-grade capture, built for kids on phones.

Status:plan rev 3 + Phase 0 memo + decisions
Date:2026-09-27
Owner decision:open sharing for everyone
Evidence:design/clips/

1. Summary

What we are building. Every game gets a clip button (a clapperboard) in the shell header.

  • While a kid plays, the game quietly keeps the last minute in memory and, where the device measures well, the whole session on disk, like Steam's background recording.
  • One tap makes a clip of the last 30 seconds: a real MP4 (H.264 video, AAC audio; WebM on older Firefox and some 32-bit Android phones). It plays and saves in iPhone Photos, iMessage, WhatsApp, Android galleries and YouTube.
  • At the end of a great run, the kid can make the whole run into a video.
  • Games make their own highlight clips (new best, win, level clear) without the kid lifting a finger.
  • Kids can trim, pick a shape, add an end card with their gamer tag, and keep a library.
  • Sharing goes through the phone's own share sheet in two taps.
  • If Jack approves Phase 7, kids can also make a web link that plays inline in iMessage and brings friends back to play.
  • If Jack approves decision 4, the board and tile games also get "Watch the run" next to leaderboard scores.

How hard. A program of about 58 pull requests in 9 phases, about 2,100 expert hours (±30%, itemized in section 16). About 430 hours of that are the optional Phase 7. The size is honest:

  • Every live-capture and board game got a code-level integration spec. Three apps get theirs in PR 4.7.
  • The research found that most games need real fixes before a clip of them is worth sharing. Several loops run at the wrong speed, 17 modules are silent, several show a wrong "new best", and two games let a kid type text that is drawn straight into the game picture.
  • Those fixes ship to every kid in Phase 1, before any clip code.
  • The genuinely unknown numbers (iPhone frame readback, phone encoder behavior, session-timeline flash writes) are measured first, in Phase 0, on Jack's devices.

What it costs to run. Phases 0-6 run on the kid's device: $0 server cost. Hosted links (Phase 7, optional), with the screener's real CPU and memory measured in a Phase 7 gate before launch (the ranges below are that uncertainty):

  • About $5-17/month at 1,000 new links a month.
  • About $8-26/month at 10,000 new links a month.
  • Worst-case abuse is bounded at about $47/month by construction. Past the per-pool caps, only unestablished identities pause, and established kids keep making links.
  • With 30-day links, the default storage cap is reached at about 37,000 new links a month. Raising it is a deliberate, costed step. Nothing turns off quietly.

2. Settled decisions and constraints

DecisionSourceWhat it means for the build
Open sharing for everyone. No grown-up gate before the share sheet, any age.Jack, 2026-09-27, verbatim: "open sharing for everyone"No age check or parent step in any share or link flow. Safety comes from what a clip can contain, never from who may share it.
10/10 capabilities, no accepted limitations, low server cost.Jack, 2026-09-27: "perfect 10/10 capabilities ... do not skimp or cut corners or accept limitations ... without killing us with server costs"Every fallback path is designed. Every cap is justified by a real constraint (storage, memory, cost) and is visible to the kid. Heavy work runs on the device.
Clips carry game pixels and game audio only. Never the microphone, camera or screen.COPPA 16 CFR 312.2 item (8)Permissions-Policy adds display-capture=(); E2E fails on any getUserMedia or getDisplayMedia call.
No kid-typed text and no gamer tag in live frames; at most a first name that is part of a game's own world (guardrail 7), pending decision 12.Guardrails 2 and 7; COPPA 312.2 item (1)Section 10. The gamer tag appears only on the end card, only if chosen.
No new paid outside services for the product.Guardrail 3Railway bucket; open-source models in a Railway service; no Cloudflare Stream or R2; no device cloud. Owner-side legal expenses (PO box, $6 DMCA fee) are Jack's, not product services.
One game = one island.Project CLAUDE.mdMachinery in shared/clips and shared/lib/audio. A game adds one hook call; DOM games add lib/clipRenderer.ts.

Proposals in this plan that are mine, not settled policy, are marked "proposal" and collected in section 19.

3. What the research proved, and what it did not

Four rounds, 35 agents:

  • Codebase mappers for every game.
  • Five web-research tracks, each attacked by an independent fact-checker.
  • Live prototypes: Chrome 153 and Safari 26.5 on this Mac, under the production CSP, against hankshits.com.
  • Two review waves on this plan.

Raw results are in design/clips/research/*.json, and prototype sources are in design/clips/prototype/. PR 0.6 re-runs every "proven" row on current releases (Chrome 155, Safari/iOS 27) and stamps the versions beside it.

ClaimStatusEvidence
Canvas, WebGL and same-origin iframe games record to real, non-blank H.264 on prod.proven (desktop Chrome)Live spike: Breakout, Monster Truck, Retro Arcade, Four-Wheeler Adventure.
Recording the raw full-resolution canvas kills frame rate.provenMonster Truck fell from about 41 fps to 4-6 fps at 2400x1728.
A small compositor fixes it within budget.not provenThe 960-wide captureStream path still cost Monster Truck 17-19% on an M5, at about 15 fps of capture. Phase 0 measures four paths (below).
A rolling "last 30 seconds" buffer with WebCodecs plus mediabunny; a 30 s clip is a packet copy.proven (desktop)12.8 ms (Chrome) and 19 ms (Safari) to mux 30 s at 720p30.
Safari's AAC encoder writes a broken decoder config (WebKit 302253), and it can be rebuilt.provenReproduced on Safari 26.5; the rebuilt config played at 48 kHz stereo.
AAC encoder delay compensated in every player.partlyAn edit list alone reads 0 ms in ffmpeg but -44 ms in AVFoundation (native) and -22.6 ms (WASM). Edit list plus sgpd/sbgp roll groups reads 0 ms in both. mediabunny does not write roll groups; the plan adds a moov patch (6.4).
Chrome's first "realtime" hardware encoder stalls 11-17 s on every browser launch.proven problemlatencyMode: 'quality' started in 26-372 ms with no reordering. Used on Chromium, plus a watchdog.
No SharedArrayBuffer (no COOP/COEP); enabling it would break the EmulatorJS CDN.provenAll transport uses transferable buffers.
Next 16.2.x production builds break new Worker(new URL(...)).provenvercel/next.js #94015, fixed in 16.3.0.
iPhone cost of reading a composited frame (WebKit does a synchronous readback for every 2D/OffscreenCanvas VideoFrame; only a WebGL HTMLCanvasElement gets a GPU copy).measured: see 3aWebKit source. Measured in Phase 0 on a real iPhone.
Phone encoder behavior: keyframe response (Chromium Android defaults MediaCodec to a 3000-frame GOP), portrait sizes, concurrent sessions, AAC delay on Android and Windows, cold start on Android and ChromeOS, Firefox's 10 s VP8 keyframe interval.unmeasuredPhase 0 measures each; the probe also runs at every arm in production.
iMessage inline-plays an og:video MP4 up to about 10 MB through a cross-origin 302.partlymacOS LinkPresentation, same-origin, localhost only. The real-phone unfurl table is a Phase 7 gate.

The compositor cost decides the capture path per engine. Phase 0 measures four paths on desktop Chrome, desktop Safari and a real iPhone:

  1. A. Main-thread 2D compositor: game frame, game-name band and host brand in one canvas, then a VideoFrame. One readback per frame on WebKit.
  2. B. Direct WebGL frame (3D games): new VideoFrame(webglCanvas), which is a GPU copy on WebKit.
    • No DOM HUD in the frame. Score goes on the end card, or the HUD is drawn inside the WebGL scene as a last pass, so the live game and the clip match.
    • The lab tests whether WebKit's encoder accepts frames larger than its configured size and scales them.
    • A rotation on this path either starts a new epoch or routes through path D.
  3. C. Lower cadence on weak devices: the lowest rungs of 6.2 at 540p, stepping up when there is headroom.
  4. D. Worker compositing: transfer VideoFrame(gameCanvas) (0.04 ms main-thread in Chrome) to the encode worker; scale and composite there in an OffscreenCanvas.

The memo states the steady-state rung each device class will settle at for each game type, so no gate is failed by design.

3a. Phase 0 decision memo: iPhone (measured 2026-09-28)

Device: iPhone SE (3rd gen, A15, 4 GB), iOS 27.0, Safari tab, 60 Hz, 2x pixel density. Driven over USB with Apple's safaridriver. The lab was injected into real hankshits.com pages (a secure page we own), so nothing was exposed to the internet and nothing was written to production. Raw data: design/clips/research/phase0-iphone-se3-2026-09-28/. Lab sources: design/clips/prototype/ilab/. Where this memo and sections 4-15 differ, this memo wins.

QuestionMeasured answerDecision
Encoder latency mode on WebKit'realtime' silently dropped about 55% of frames whenever the GPU was busy, even with a light 3D scene holding a steady 60 fps (88-95 of 210 frames out; no errors, no queue drops). 'quality' delivered 210/210 in every run, 0 frames out of order across 6 runs, keyframes honored at 1 s, first output after 19-90 ms (no cold-start stall on iOS).latencyMode: 'quality' on every engine (Chromium already needed it for its 11-17 s realtime stall). Keep the reordering assertion as a runtime check.
2D canvas games: cost of taking a frameAfter the game draws (path D): 1-4 ms for small canvases (Breakout 480x640 2-3 ms, Flappy 1-2 ms, Hill Climb 2 ms, all with 0% fps loss), but 11-15 ms for a big canvas (Four-Wheeler Adventure 1334x526: 26% fps loss). A smaller compositor (18% loss) and createImageBitmap (0 ms sync, still 23% loss) do not help: the read forces Safari to finish the game's pending drawing on the spot.Path P: capture the previous, already-presented frame at the start of the next rAF dispatch, before the game draws. Measured 2-3 ms for the 1334x526 canvas (6.8% loss at 15 fps, 11.4% at 30 fps while the phone was heat-throttled). One frame of latency; 2D canvases keep their pixels between frames.
WebGL (R3F) games: cost of taking a framenew VideoFrame(webglCanvas) forces a CPU-GPU sync of 23-30 ms. Monster Truck lost 27-38% fps at any capture rate from 10 to 30 fps. Path A (2D compositor) was worst (21-26 ms, 10% loss even on a synthetic scene).Path E: asynchronous GPU readback on the game's own WebGL2 context. After the render, in the same task: resolve-blit the drawing buffer, scale-and-flip blit to 640 wide, readPixels into a pixel-buffer object, then fenceSync. Pick the pixels up once the fence signals (it was always ready by the next frame). Measured main-thread cost 0 ms median, 1 ms worst 5%. Monster Truck lost about 8% (partly heat); Four-Wheeler 3D lost 0%. 287/293 frames captured and encoded, 0 GL errors, and snapshots verified as correct upright game frames. All GL state is saved and restored (three.js caches bindings).
Zero-code capture timingThe requestAnimationFrame dispatcher (game callbacks, then capture, in one task) worked on all six live games tested, including the iframe game, without touching game code.Keep auto-discovery as designed; path P or E is chosen from the canvas's context type.
60 fps capture2D synthetic game at 60 fps capture: 0% loss, 3-4 ms per frame, 420/420 frames out.60 fps target is viable for 2D games on A15-class iPhones; the governor still decides per device.
AAC on iOS 27Native AudioEncoder present. Encoder delay 2114 samples (same as macOS). WebKit 302253 is still live (raw esds bytes returned); mediabunny's rebuild yields ASC 1190. A 30 s clip muxed in 12 ms; with a fresh audio encode, 146 ms. Two encoder instances produced byte-identical avcC.As planned: continuous AAC, always rebuild the ASC, splice title cards by packet copy after an avcC byte compare.
A/V sync of iPhone-made filesWith an edit list only: ffmpeg -0.6 ms, AVFoundation -44 ms at every beep. After adding sgpd/sbgp roll groups (ffmpeg remux, no sample changes): AVFoundation 0 ms, ffmpeg -0.6 ms.The roll-group moov patch (6.4) is required and proven on real iPhone output.
Storage in a Safari tabQuota 38.7 GB. persist() returns false. OPFS sync handle in a worker: about 2.5 GB/s write, 6 ms flush, about 6.7 GB/s read. canShare accepts video/mp4 files.Session timeline and Record are not IO-bound. The Safari 7-day eviction applies in tabs, so the Save to Photos coaching stays.
Screen wake locknavigator.wakeLock.request('screen') is refused without a user gesture.Request it inside the Play tap.
User agent"iPhone OS 18_7" with "Version/27.0".Confirms: never parse versions; feature-probe only.

Also found:

  • Monster Truck and Four-Wheeler 3D already run at only 33-38 fps on this iPhone before any recording (#35i).
  • Hill Climb draws at 1x on a 2x screen (PR 1.7).
  • After about 30 minutes of back-to-back tests the phone heat-throttled: Four-Wheeler Adventure's baseline fell from 60 to 49 fps, so the thermal soak in section 15 is a real gate.
  • In automation, WebDriver taps did not trigger the start card's Play on two pages while they worked on others. This is an automation artifact to re-check with a real finger, not a product change.

Still to measure (section 16, PR 0.6 list):

  • Retro Arcade (EmulatorJS is WebGL1: path E needs WebGL2, and path P does not apply to WebGL, so it needs its own test).
  • The audio tap inside a real game, and the ringer switch.
  • The iOS share-size ceiling.
  • The Home Screen app.
  • Memory and thermal soaks.
  • Android (the SM-X230 tablet is attached but untouched pending Jack's OK), a Chromebook, and desktop Safari.

4. Architecture

flowchart LR
  subgraph Main["Main thread (game page)"]
    G[Game: canvas / R3F / iframe / DOM log] -->|same-task frame hook| P[FramePump]
    GA[Shared game-audio bus] --> TAP[AudioWorklet tap, before the speaker gain]
    CS[ClipService singleton] --- UI[Clip button, toasts, editor, My Clips]
    GOV[Governor] --- P
  end
  subgraph EW["Encode worker"]
    CMP[Compositor path D] --> VE
    VE[VideoEncoder H.264] --> VR[(Video GOP ring 60 s)]
    MIX[Mixer: per-stream PLL + resampler] --> AE[AAC encoder native or WASM] --> AR[(AAC ring)]
    MIX --> PCM[(PCM ring 62 s)]
  end
  subgraph IO["IO worker"]
    MUX[mediabunny mux + roll-group patch] --> OPFS[(OPFS clips + IndexedDB rows)]
    TL[Session timeline journal, one part per epoch] --> OPFS
    ED[Editor render pass] --> OPFS
  end
  P -->|VideoFrame transfer| CMP
  P -->|path A frames| VE
  TAP -->|MessagePort PCM| MIX
  VE -->|tee packets| TL
  AE -->|tee packets| TL
  VR -->|clip: copy GOPs| MUX
  AR --> MUX
  OPFS --> SH[Share sheet / download / hosted link]

Main: game -> FramePump (same task) -> VideoFrame transfer -> encode worker (optional compositing, path D) -> VideoEncoder -> GOP ring
Main: shared audio bus -> tap (before the speaker gain) -> MessagePort -> mixer -> AAC encoder -> AAC ring; PCM ring
Encode worker tees packets -> IO worker session timeline (one journal part per encoder epoch)
Clip: GOPs + AAC -> IO worker mux + roll-group patch -> OPFS + IndexedDB -> share / download / link

4.1 Module layout (under apps/web/src/shared/clips/ unless noted)

PathResponsibility
ClipService.tsTab singleton outside React (survives client route changes). State machine, source registry, owner key, Web Locks election so only the focused tab captures.
config.ts + app/api/clips-config/route.tsRuntime flag CLIPS_MODE (off|dogfood|on), read by a dynamic no-store route that also checks the signed dogfood cookie. The route sits outside the /api/clips* prefix that the Phase 7 origin lock guards.
  • Capture UI and workers load through dynamic import() only when capture is enabled.
  • The service worker caches the last verdict for 7 days. The kill switch stops capture but never hides the kid's library: My Clips always opens, offline too.
  • Code default before release: on in development (kid clones, local dev), off in production unless the variable is set. PR 6.2 flips the production default to on, so kid clones deployed with put-it-online get clips with no setup; CLIPS_MODE=off stays as the kill switch.
ClipProvider.tsxMounted by GameShell for any module whose metadata literal is clips: true (plus the scrub gate for kid-built or kid-changed modules, section 10). Uses the useSyncExternalStore mounted pattern.
sources/useClipSource, useR3FClipSource, useIframeClipSource, useReplaySource, autoDiscover.ts, markMoment, runPhase.
runtime/capabilities.ts (probes in window and worker scope; cache keyed on a hash of the probe results plus the opaque user-agent string, never parsed), framePump.ts, captureClock.ts, compositor.ts, governor.ts, frameMeter.ts.

library/, ui/, replay/IndexedDB rows and owner partitions; all UI (every layer above z-1000 portals to document.body); the DOM-replay kit.
shared/lib/audio/gameAudio.tsOne AudioContext, per-game channels, a speaker gain AFTER the tap point, a limiter, a document-level unlock listener, the iframe constructor shim.
public/clips/Static same-origin files: tap worklet, WASM AAC encoder and decoder (SIMD and scalar), license texts.

5. Device tiers and fallbacks

The support floor is the site's own browser target (Next 16: Safari 16.4+, Chrome 111+, Firefox 111+). Inside it, every device gets clips, chosen by probes that re-run at every arm.

TierWho lands hereVideoAudioReplay granularity
WChrome/Edge/Samsung on Windows, macOS, Android arm64; every iOS browser on iOS 26+; macOS Safari 26+VideoEncoder H.264, latencyMode 'quality' on every engine (3a: WebKit 'realtime' silently drops frames)AudioEncoder AAC-LC, ASC always rebuilt, 1024 samples of silence pre-padded at every configure (Safari drops the first 1024 real samples)1 s
W+iOS and macOS Safari 16.4-25.x; ChromeOS and Linux Chrome (no native AAC); Firefox desktop 130+Same as WFFmpeg aacenc WASM in the encode worker. These devices also lack AudioData (and iOS lacks AudioDecoder): raw PCM plus the WASM decoder.1 s
MVideoEncoder probe fails, MediaRecorder offers video/mp4;codecs=avc1,mp4a.40.2MediaRecorder on the compositor canvas; videoKeyFrameIntervalDuration where supported; observed keyframe spacing probed at armSame recorderMeasured, shown to the kid
VFirefox Android; Firefox desktop 111-129; 32-bit ARM Chrome without a hardware H.264 encoderMediaRecorder VP8 WebM as rotating recorders (each restart begins with a keyframe and its own header; two overlap only across the hand-off; mediabunny concatenates)OpusRotation period N, measured on real Firefox Android in Phase 0
  • Tier V files are labeled honestly (video/webm, .webm). 32-bit Chrome Android shares them through the share sheet; Firefox uses Save to phone.
  • Tier V links: the Phase 7 screener transcodes accepted WebM to H.264/AAC MP4, sized to the 9 MB inline target. That is server-side encoding, not distribution, and it is capped per identity.
  • WASM H.264 encoding is rejected (Appendix B).

5.1 Encoder configuration (W and W+)

  • Codec level is computed from width, height and fps: 3.1 (1f) for 720p30, 3.2 (20) for 720p60, 4.0 (28) for 1080p. Profile order is High, Main, Baseline. Probe prefer-hardware first. If only software passes, the session starts on the software preset (one size step down, at most 30 fps). iOS ignores hardwareAcceleration, so probes drive everything.
  • Per-source frame-rate target: 60 fps for fast 2D games that request it where the device measures headroom, otherwise 30.
    • Bitrates (VBR): 2.0 Mbps at 30 fps and 3.5 Mbps at 60 fps for 2D and pixel art; 3.0 Mbps for 3D; 1.0 Mbps for DOM replays (offline).
    • AAC: new Quality({ bitrate: 128000 }).
  • Color: colorSpace is set explicitly to BT.709 limited range, with a matching colr box. A color-bar lab pattern must decode within delta-E 2000 of 3 or less in ffmpeg and AVFoundation, for each encoder family (VideoToolbox, MediaCodec, Media Foundation, OpenH264).
  • Keyframes every 1 s by request, verified on output (chunk.type plus an IDR NAL check).
    • A keyframe-response probe runs at arm. If a device ignores keyframe requests, the fallback forces a new session with reset() plus configure() (an identical configure() is a no-op in Chromium). That is an epoch boundary (6.6).
    • The ring is also capped by bytes inside a GOP. If that cap would cut the only GOP, the clip starts with a re-encoded leading partial GOP.
  • Coded frame size is constant for the whole session and even. Rotation letterboxes inside it (paths A, C, D). Governor resolution steps scale content inside the same coded frame, so the SPS never changes size. Android encoders that reject portrait sizes get landscape-coded frames with rotation metadata (probed).
  • Cold start: no output 2.5 s after the first encode starts a bridge encoder on Chromium desktop and Android arm64. The bridge runs at the session's coded size; the software preset caps it at 20 fps. The hand-over back to hardware is an epoch boundary. WebKit and 32-bit ARM Chrome have no software H.264, so no bridge: they show Warming until output arrives.
  • Splices: every spliced or epoch-boundary GOP has at least 2 frames, so back-to-back IDRs from two encoders never share idr_pic_id (H.264 7.4.3). A Node fixture parses slice headers at each splice.

6. Capture, encode and audio pipeline

6.1 Capture sources

SourceHow a frame is takenModules
2D canvasuseClipSource({ canvasRef, paintHud?, fps?, isPlaying? }). The shell's rAF dispatcher captures the previous, already-presented frame before the game draws (path P, 3a); games only register the canvas and HUD. fps is the capture target; the simulation rate is a separate simHz.arkanoid, asteroids, blitz-bomber, bomberman, breakout, dino-runner, endless-runner, flappy-bird, hextris, hill-climb, math-attack, platformer, space-invaders
R3F / WebGLuseR3FClipSource() inside <Canvas> using R3F addAfterEffect (after render, same task) to start an asynchronous GPU readback on the game's WebGL2 context (path E, 3a). Never useFrame, which runs before render, and never new VideoFrame(webglCanvas), which forces a 23-30 ms GPU sync on iPhone.monster-truck, four-wheeler-3d (main canvas registered explicitly)
Same-origin iframeA prelude installed before the game script wraps the realm's requestAnimationFrame, so capture runs after the game's draw. Timestamps convert through performance.timeOrigin.four-wheeler-adventure, retro-arcade (EmulatorJS pinned)
DOM replayThe module logs state; clips render offline (section 9).2048, checkers, chess, cookie-clicker, memory-match, quoridor, snake, wordle, oregon-trail, trivia, drum-machine, virtual-pet
Auto-discoveryFor kid-built canvas games with no hook, the shell wraps requestAnimationFrame so game callbacks run first and then the pump, in the same frame task, on the largest canvas in the play area. The make-a-game scaffold uses useGameLoop for kid-built 2D canvas games. The game-contract test fails on zero captured frames, which catches a game that renders from a timer.Future games

Clip shape. At session start the compositor picks Tall (9:16) or Wide (16:9) from the source aspect. Live frames hold the game region plus the game-name band. In the editor, changing shape makes a new copy, with letterbox or crop, and says so. The original stays.

6.2 Frame pacing and timestamps

  • The display refresh is measured as the median of the shortest rAF intervals and snapped to a standard rate (50, 60, 75, 90, 120, 144, 165 Hz). The capture stride is k = ceil(Hz / targetFps), which never exceeds the target.
  • Timestamps come from time, not a counter: slot = round((rafTs - epochStart - removedPauseTime) / (k * vsyncPeriod)). An empty slot (a missed vsync, or a backpressure drop) extends the previous sample's duration. Two captures in one slot keep the later one. A 41 fps game on a 60 Hz screen produces video as long as its audio.
  • No frameRate is passed to the muxer, so mediabunny uses its 57600 time scale (rounding under 9 µs).
  • At most 2 frames are in flight. Backpressure drops a frame (counted), never queues it.
RefreshGovernor rungs, 30 fps target (fps = Hz / k, floor 15)Rungs, 60 fps target
50 Hz25, 16.750, 25, 16.7
60 Hz30, 20, 1560, 30, 20, 15
75 Hz25, 18.8, 1537.5, 25, 18.8, 15
90 Hz30, 22.5, 18, 1545, 30, 22.5, 18, 15
120 Hz30, 24, 20, 17.1, 1560, 40, 30, 24, 20, 17.1, 15
144 Hz28.8, 24, 20.6, 18, 1648, 36, 28.8, 24, 20.6, 18, 16
165 Hz27.5, 23.6, 20.6, 18.3, 16.5, 1555, 41.2, 33, 27.5, 23.6, 20.6, 18.3, 16.5, 15

6.3 Audio: one bus for every game

There are 12 first-party AudioContext sites, 1 in the four-wheeler srcdoc iframe, and EmulatorJS's own. The class fix:

  1. One context: getGameAudio() creates one AudioContext. Its unlock is a document-level capture-phase listener (pointerdown, touchend, keydown) installed on first import, so apps with no start card unlock too. GameStartOverlay's Play also calls unlock() synchronously.
  2. Graph: channels, then master, then the tap point, then the speaker gain (every sound toggle drives this), then the limiter, then the destination. Clips keep game sound when a kid plays muted. The editor's "Game sounds" switch decides the final mix.
  3. No closing: the context is typed BaseAudioContext; games call channel.dispose(), never close().
  4. Iframe realms get a constructor shim (prepended to the srcdoc, inlined in the emulator page) that redirects destination to a bus in that realm. This is the only Retro Arcade audio mechanism. The shim is a pure pass-through until ClipService arms.
  5. Lazy tap: the tap worklet attaches when ClipService arms (behind the flag). Phase 1 adds no always-on cost.
  6. Enforcement: ESLint bans new AudioContext, webkitAudioContext, .destination and new Audio( under src/games/** and src/apps/**, and a source-scan test covers public/games/**/*.html.
  7. Kept out of clips: UI sounds (on a UI node that never reaches the tap) and read-aloud.
  8. Ringer switch: if WebKit delivers silence to the tap with the iPhone ringer on silent (measured in Phase 0), the clip is labeled "No game sounds in this clip" with a one-tap music bed offer.

6.4 Audio into the encoder

  • Tap transport: the tap worklet converts to Int16 stereo and posts 2048-frame batches over a transferred MessagePort. Buffers are recycled, so the audio thread never allocates.
  • Anchoring: every stream anchors on production time (the worklet's currentFrame/currentTime with performance.now() in the same task every 250 ms and on state changes), converted through performance.timeOrigin for iframe realms. It never uses getOutputTimestamp, because Bluetooth latency would leak into the recording.
  • Pauses are removed in the PCM domain before the encoder, with 5-10 ms fades and a contiguous sample counter. No packet is ever dropped mid-stream.
  • Silence is synthesized in real time for any live span with no audio.
  • AAC is encoded continuously by calling AudioEncoder or the WASM encoder directly; mediabunny only muxes.
  • Priming P: calibrated per session. Audio timestamps shift by -P/48000. The IO worker patches the finished moov with an edit list plus sgpd/sbgp roll groups (proposed upstream as mediabunny #444) for clips, timeline parts, Record and exports.
  • Players that ignore edit lists: the first audio packet is chosen so that playback is at most 25 ms early there. A Node row (ffprobe -ignore_editlist 1) and an ExoPlayer 2.x row in the Android emulator check it, with a +45 ms tolerance.
  • Clip bounds: a clip ends at min(video end, AAC watermark).
  • Tests: a box-writer unit test, and a Node fixture that must read within 5 ms in both ffprobe and avcheck.swift.

6.4.1 Mixer

Each tap stream (the page bus, each iframe realm) has its own anchor, phase-locked loop and resampler to 48 kHz. The loop drives the resampling ratio, so drift never becomes timestamp jumps. The worker mixes with clamping. A per-stream watermark drops a stream the moment it is suspended or closed, or its realm unloads, and zero-fills it. A Bluetooth-route A/V check is in the thresholds.

6.5 Rings and memory class

  • Rings: the video GOP ring (60 s, epoch-tagged), the AAC ring (about 1 MB/min) and the PCM ring (Int16, 62 s, 11.5 MB).
  • Memory class:
    • Chromium uses navigator.deviceMemory.
    • WebKit uses a conservative default: iPhones with 'AudioEncoder' in self (iOS 26+, A13 or newer) get the middle class. Every iPad starts in the lowest class, because iPadOS 26 also runs on 2-3 GB A12 iPads. Older iOS gets the lowest class.
    • The lowest class has a 30 s ring. The Phase 0 soak sizes every class.
    • A budget trim that cuts coverage shows the real seconds on the button.
  • Export memory budget: per class, NV12 decode frames plus one export encoder: lowest 60 MB, middle 120 MB, high 200 MB. Measured in Phase 0 and enforced by chunked decode.

6.6 Clip assembly and the epoch policy

  • Clip it! starts at the last keyframe at or before the requested start (at most 1 s longer). The video track has no edit list. The editor makes frame-exact starts by re-encoding only the first partial GOP.
  • Epoch sources: recovery, iOS resume, bridge hand-over, forced keyframe sessions, and export sessions.
  • Epoch boundaries are classified by a byte compare of the avcC and coded size:
    • Identical: splice by packet copy.
    • Different: re-encoding the older part needs a decoder and an encoder beside the live one. So at clip time during play, the clip is cut to the newest epoch, and the toast states its real length.
    • At the next break, "Make it longer" re-encodes the older part (an export session). It also runs at once when the Phase 0 probe shows the device supports three concurrent sessions.
    Never silent.
  • The same rule applies to the session timeline and Record (8.3): one track is never written across two configs.
  • Node fixture: two different avcCs must decode in ffmpeg and AVFoundation.
  • Muxing runs in the IO worker. GOP buffers are copied in, so a slow phone mux never stalls frame intake.
  • Every clip and highlight is written to OPFS the moment it is built.
  • Mash protection: a tap within 5 s of the last clip extends that clip ("Clip made! (longer)"). Test: tap, tap within 5 s, then tap after 6 s gives two library rows.

7. Performance governor and lifecycle

The guarantee: capture never costs the game more than 10% of its frame rate. It is enforced at runtime, at the rung Phase 0 predicts for that device class.

  • Signals: primary signals are capture-attributed (capture cost p95 against the frame budget, encoder queue and latency, frames that only capture pushed over budget, and Chrome's Long Animation Frames attribution). Frame rate against a baseline is only the severe backstop.
  • Ladder: the rungs in 6.2, then content scaled down inside the coded frame, then low-power capture (lowest rung, last 15 s kept), then resting. Step up after 30 s clean, at most twice per session.
  • Resting is honest: a clip from the pre-rest buffer says "This clip is from before the clip button rested." Record stays available at the low-power rung.
  • Extra gates: Compute Pressure "serious" steps down and "critical" rests; battery under 20% and discharging rests (Chrome); iOS Low Power Mode is inferred from a 30 Hz rAF on a 60+ Hz device.
  • Crash-loop breaker: each tab holds a Web Lock for its lifetime, and a marker counts as a crash only if its lock is gone. One crash starts the game one rung lower. Two in 7 days turn replay off for that game (the DISABLED state, with its reason). Five clean sessions reset it.
  • Export sessions: while the editor or "Make it longer" exports (the game is paused), the live encoder is flushed and closed, so a phone never needs three codec sessions. Play resumes on a new epoch. Phase 0 probes the maximum concurrent sessions on Android. An Android E2E runs an export during a pause.
stateDiagram-v2
  [*] --> IDLE
  IDLE --> WARMING: source registered
  WARMING --> BUFFERING: output + calibration ok
  WARMING --> BRIDGED: no output after 2.5 s (Chromium only)
  BRIDGED --> BUFFERING: hardware ready (new epoch)
  BUFFERING --> RECORDING: Record
  RECORDING --> BUFFERING: Stop (not rested)
  RECORDING --> RESTING: Stop (session was rested)
  RECORDING --> RESTING: governor severe (keeps recording at low-power rung)
  RECORDING --> SUSPENDED: hidden (fragment finalized)
  RECORDING --> RECOVERING: encoder error (part closed)
  RECORDING --> SOURCE_LOST: canvas gone (part finalized)
  BUFFERING --> RESTING: governor severe
  RESTING --> RECORDING: Record (low-power rung)
  RESTING --> BUFFERING: probe passes
  BUFFERING --> SUSPENDED: hidden / paused / start card
  SUSPENDED --> BUFFERING: visible + playing
  BUFFERING --> EXPORTING: export (encoder closed)
  EXPORTING --> BUFFERING: done (new epoch)
  BUFFERING --> SOURCE_LOST: canvas gone
  SOURCE_LOST --> BUFFERING: re-registered within 1.5 s
  SOURCE_LOST --> IDLE: grace over (ring kept 5 min in OPFS tmp, then purged)
  BUFFERING --> RECOVERING: encoder error
  RECOVERING --> BUFFERING: recreated (new epoch)
  RECOVERING --> DISABLED: 4 failures in 60 s, or breaker
  IDLE --> RECORD_ONLY: device fallback (e.g. Retro Arcade on iPhone)
  BUFFERING --> IDLE: owner change (purge rules)

IDLE -> WARMING -> BUFFERING; WARMING -> BRIDGED -> BUFFERING; BUFFERING <-> RECORDING, RESTING, SUSPENDED, EXPORTING, SOURCE_LOST, RECOVERING; RECORDING -> RESTING/SUSPENDED/RECOVERING/SOURCE_LOST; SOURCE_LOST -> IDLE after grace; RECOVERING -> DISABLED; IDLE -> RECORD_ONLY fallback; owner change -> IDLE

7.1 Lifecycle rules

  • Visible pauses keep the encoder alive.
  • iOS hidden: flush and close at once. Errors in the first seconds after resume do not count toward the disable limit.
  • Owner changes: keep the guest ring only when sign-in completes within 60 s of that run's end, in the same tab (the same kid saving the run they just played, from any sign-in entry point). Otherwise purge it. Always purge on user-to-other-user, on user-to-guest, and on bfcache restore when the owner differs.
  • Web Locks election: only the focused tab captures.

8. Library, session timeline and Record

8.1 Library

  • Where it lives: bytes in OPFS through a SyncAccessHandle in the IO worker; rows, thumbnails and edit recipes in IndexedDB.
  • Write protocol:
    1. Write to a temp file.
    2. Verify it: it parses, the duration is within 0.2 s, and the first packet is a keyframe.
    3. Move it into place.
    4. Write the IndexedDB row under a Web Lock.
    5. Tell other tabs over BroadcastChannel.
  • Fallbacks: private windows go straight to an in-memory clip with a sticky "Share it or save it to your phone now!" sheet. Non-private OPFS failures fall back to IndexedDB, storing ArrayBuffer chunks.
  • Owner partitions: ownerKey is guest or a salted hash of the user id. Sign-out hides clips and never deletes them. After sign-in, a poster grid with a checkbox per clip asks "Which of these are yours?", with read-aloud. Nothing moves without a tap.
  • Budget: at most 25% of the quota estimate. Where estimate() is missing (Safari before 17), a write-then-truncate probe measures the space. QuotaExceededError is a normal stop with kid-word copy.
  • No time-based deletion. Auto clips and unkept timeline parts are evicted only when the library passes its budget: oldest unkept first, never an unwatched (NEW) clip. The kid first sees what stays, with a spoken notice. Clips the kid made, Record videos and pictures are never auto-deleted.
  • Offline: sw.js caches the /clips shell, runtime chunks, worklet, WASM and the last flag verdict. My Clips plays, trims and shares offline.
  • New device: an empty library on a device that has never made a clip shows "Clips live on the device you played on. Ones you saved are in your Photos or gallery." with read-aloud.

8.2 Eviction on iPhone

Safari deletes all script-written storage, including OPFS, after 7 days of Safari use with no visit, unless the site is on the Home Screen.

  • Every share step offers Save to Photos, with a picture pointing at "Save Video".
  • My Clips in a Safari tab says "Your clips live in Safari. Save favorites to Photos!"
  • IOSInstallPrompt gets a reason="clips" variant: save to Photos first, because the Home Screen app has separate storage and separate cookies. It never shows during play (#32i).
  • In-app browsers show "Open in Safari to keep your clips" before the first save.
  • A server-set HttpOnly counter cookie lets the library say once, honestly, that the browser cleaned up old clips.
  • persist() is called silently on Chromium and WebKit after the first kept clip, never during play, and never on Firefox, which gets an explain-first button in settings.

8.3 Session timeline (Steam-style background recording)

  • What it records: the encode worker tees the same encoded packets into a fragmented-MP4 journal in OPFS. There is no second encoder. Media bytes are flushed before the journal.
  • One part per epoch: at every avcC or coded-size change the journal rolls to a new part (6.6 classification). Joining parts at "Make the whole run a video":
    • Identical parts: spliced by packet copy.
    • A different part: re-encoded in an export session at that break (the smaller part is re-encoded). If that is impossible, the video starts at the newest compatible part, and the sheet states its real start.
    A Node fixture journals a mid-run different-avcC epoch and must decode in both decoder families.
  • Segments and markers: the journal is segmented by runPhase and marked with markMoment stars. At the end of a run longer than the ring, the result chip offers "Make the whole run a video (4:12)". The editor filmstrip scrubs the whole session.
  • Budget: library headroom only, no fixed minute count. The only exception is a cap the Phase 0 flash-write measurement justifies, stated in settings with its reason. When a run is longer than what the timeline still holds, the offer says its true start ("from 2:10 into your run").
  • Cleanup: unkept parts are deleted at session end, or under budget pressure. Never silently.
  • Default: the timeline defaults on only for device classes that pass the flash-write and battery measurements. Elsewhere settings says "This device keeps the last minute", and a whole-run video is offered only when Record was on.

8.4 Record mode

  • Record marks the timeline segment to keep from the tap on, with the same epoch rules.
  • While recording, the clip button becomes the red stop control in the same single header slot. A timer pill and a star (bookmark) button sit in the in-play toast slot under the header, in a reserved position that per-game controls must avoid.
  • Library budget: Record respects it. Near the budget, the pill turns amber and shows a filling bar, and a spoken warning plays. At the budget, Record stops and keeps the video ("Video made!").
  • Crash recovery: on the next load, truncate to the last durable fragment, remux, and show "We saved your recording from last time!" Files whose Web Lock a live tab holds are skipped.
  • Size-aware sharing (per platform):
    • Ceilings: Chromium caps shares at 50 MiB. WebKit reads a shared file into memory twice, so Phase 0 measures the largest file that shares without a memory kill on the oldest supported iPhone.
    • Above the ceiling, the kid picks: Trim; a smaller copy (only if at least 1.2 Mbps at 720p fits); split into parts under the ceiling; "Save to Files" (iPhone, this case only, coached); or a link (Phase 7).
    • navigator.share is never called above the ceiling.
    • A named test shares the largest clip a kid can make on the reference iPhone.

9. DOM-replay engine

No phone browser can screen-record page elements. These modules record what happened and render the clip offline, faster than real time.

  • Log: a compact snapshot or event per move (per tick for Snake, per click burst for Cookie Clicker). Recording the AI's choice and 2048's random tile removes any need for seeded randomness.
    • "Recent games" are kept by bytes within the library budget, not by count; logs are kilobytes, so hundreds fit.
    • My Clips shows how many are kept.
  • Renderer: src/games/<id>/lib/clipRenderer.ts draws the board with move animations.
    • Colors come from the live board's computed styles at capture time.
    • A parity test renders one logged state live (Playwright) and through the renderer, and compares geometry and palette.
  • Audio goes through the same encoder module as live capture.
  • Units: Snake and Cookie Clicker clip "the last 30 seconds". Turn-based games clip "your last 10 moves" or "whole game, sped up to fit 60 s".
  • Brag formats beside video:
    • Wordle: the spoiler-free emoji grid and "mystery tiles".
    • Cookie Clicker: a time-lapse and a stat card.
    • Trivia: the question card, the answer reveal and the streak.
    • Drum Machine: the pattern grid lighting up with bus audio.
    • Virtual Pet: care moments.
  • Watch the run (decision 4a): the Zod-validated move log rides with a leaderboard score and renders in the viewer's browser. It costs no bucket storage and uploads no pixels.
  • Live-board fixes where the clip must match: 2048 tiles teleport today, and Wordle has no reveal animation.

10. Privacy by construction

Open sharing is safe only if no clip can carry personal information. The research found two shipped games where it can today. Each class gets fixed.

11. Experience spec

The UX research (Steam, Switch, Xbox, PS5, Medal, NVIDIA Highlights, Roblox Moments, Wordle, NN/g) is in the research JSON. This section wins wherever the research copy deck disagrees.

11.1 Principles

  1. One tap, instant, and a tap always means "clip" during play.
    • On pointerdown the ring bounds are snapshotted. The clip is committed on pointerup before 500 ms, so a slow 6-year-old press still captures the moment they meant.
    • A hold of 500 ms or more without movement opens the Capture menu and commits nothing. The menu's first row, "Clip the last 30 seconds", uses the snapshot.
    • Drag off to cancel, like a native iOS button: a press released more than 48 px outside the clip button commits nothing. A release inside the button, or within 48 px of its edges, still clips, also after the finger moved. The release point is the last pointermove of the press (createPointerTrail), never the pointerup's own point: iPhone Safari can report a pointerup at (0, 0), which made every tap a drag off (iPhone SE, 2026-10-01). A cancelled press (pointercancel, a press given up for a sheet) never clips, whatever its length.
    • A tap within 5 s of the last clip extends it.
    • Test: a 600 ms hold opens the menu and leaves zero new library rows.
  2. Never interrupt play. During play the only feedback is a compact confirmation in the header row (a check mark and a small pill). The full card waits for a break.
  3. A clip is always reachable.
    • After a manual clip, a small "new clip" chip (its own 44 px target, in the in-play toast slot under the header) shows until the clip is watched. The clip button itself never changes meaning.
    • Tapping the chip opens the newest clip:
      • In games that can pause, including Monster Truck, both Four-Wheelers and Retro Arcade, which all gain a real pause in Phase 3, the game pauses first.
      • In run-based games that cannot pause (Flappy Bird, Dino Runner, Endless Runner, Hill Climb, Cookie Clicker), a tap during a run says "Your clip is ready when this run ends!" with read-aloud, and the clip opens at the run's end.
  4. Suggest, don't decide. The editor opens on a suggested 15 s window around the best moment. "All" widens it.
  5. Every drag has a tap twin.
    • Length chips and "Start here / End here" are primary. Tapping a filmstrip frame moves the playhead.
    • Handles have a 48x96 px touch box and merge into one window handle when they would overlap. Clips can be as short as 3 s, which is why the tap controls carry the load.
  6. Two taps to share, file always ready first. The editor pre-renders in the background.
  7. Read-aloud where it matters. Every sheet and empty state has a ReadAloudButton. Each editor panel has its own short spoken script (sheet title, main action, the open panel's controls), snapshot-tested like PauseMenu.test.

11.2 Header: fits at every width, in every state

Three header states: no clip button (flag off, or modules with clips off), clip button idle, and recording (the stop control takes the same single slot). PR 1.5 adds a sized, empty clip-slot prop so all three states are testable in Phase 1. The header background becomes solid (the current backdrop-blur-md is a glassmorphism tell, and it also traps fixed-position children).

Steps:

  1. Below 480 px: the cluster gap goes to 0 (hit areas stay 44 px).
  2. Below 480 px: the title becomes the game's emoji. GameShell gets an emoji prop from metadata; routes without an appId pass one. The full name stays in aria-label, the start card and the pause menu.
  3. Below 400 px, games that support pause: Leaderboard and Restart leave the header. This keys on capability, not the current canPause, so the header never reflows at run boundaries. Both live in the pause menu during play, and in the result chip at game over, where there is no pause menu.
  4. Still over budget: Sign In drops its label.
  5. Below 340 px: Fullscreen moves into the pause menu, or into the result chip for games that cannot pause.

Designed widths (px-3 padding 24, home 44, emoji title 44 with its padding, 44 px per control, gap 0): the worst case is a guest in a game that cannot pause, with Leaderboard, Fullscreen, Restart, the clip slot and the Sign In icon. That is 24 + 44 + 44 + 5 x 44 = 332 px in every state. So step 5 applies below 340 px, which brings 320 px screens to 288 px.

Tests and rules:

  • The header matrix covers 320, 360, 375, 390, 412, 430 and 480 px portrait, plus 844x390 landscape, in all three states, guest and signed in.
  • No game control that takes taps may sit inside the header box, or in the reserved toast-slot area during play.
  • Monster Truck's TILT and CALIBRATE move below both (PR 1.5). The four-wheeler-3d 13 px title override goes.
  • A mobile-playability check covers every game, including that Leaderboard is reachable at game over at 375 and 390 px.

Keyboard and gamepad:

  • Alt+C (Option+C), Alt+R, F8. The handler requires no Ctrl or Meta (AltGr), listens inside same-origin iframes, and is gated by keyBelongsToTarget.
  • Gamepad: buttons[17] on Switch Pro and Xbox Series (by gamepad.id). Elsewhere, hold button 8 for 1 s. Never in Retro Arcade.

11.3 Clip button states

StateLookTap does
WarmingRing fills over 3 s; a tap pulses it once (no motion with reduced motion)"Play a little first! Then tap the clip button again." with a tappable 🔊

Toasts that answer the kid's own tap (warming, error, resting, disabled, the "ready when this run ends" chip reply) keep a 44 px 🔊 button that takes taps even during play. Other in-play toasts take no taps.

11.4 Surfaces and where things live

  • Stacking contract (written into ARCHITECTURE.md). Every layer above z-1000 portals to document.body.
    • OrientationWarning: z-100.
    • Header row and in-play confirmation: z-1000.
    • In-play toast slot (new-clip chip, Record pill and star, tap replies): z-1050, directly under the header, outside game control areas.
    • Toast lane: z-1100, between runs.
    • Result chip: z-1200.
    • LeaderboardModal: z-1500.
    • PauseMenu: z-2000. It gains a visible "🎬 Clips" button through its children slot and spokenExtras (fixing the bug that pause-menu children are never spoken).
    • Clip sheets and editor: z-2500.
    • RestartConfirmationDialog: z-3000.
  • Result chip: one shared component over every game's result or game-over card, and the Capture home for games that cannot pause. It holds:
    • The game's read-aloud button (fixed for every game in PR 1.3).
    • Leaderboard and Restart when the header dropped them.
    • After Phase 2 (decision 24): the run's own clips. A run of 30 s or less gets one button, "Watch the whole run (m:ss)". A longer run gets "Watch the end" (its last 30 s) and "Make the whole run a video (m:ss)". Both clip bounds come from the run's own start and end on the capture timeline (runPhase), so a clip never holds an earlier run or the start card. Record a video and Take a picture stay in the Capture menu.
  • Capture menu (hold, right-click, pause menu, result chip): Clip the last 30 seconds, Record a video, Take a picture, My clips from this game, Clip settings. After the third clip, a one-time spoken tip teaches the hold.
  • Editor:
    • Layout: full screen on phones, a two-column dialog on desktop.
    • Trim tab: chips, tap twins, stars, and smart-cut starts.
    • Style tab: shape (a new copy, stated), end card, name on this clip, preset stickers, speed, Take a picture.
    • Sound tab: game sounds, and in-house synthesized music that ducks the game to 50% (rendered in a main-thread OfflineAudioContext and handed to the IO worker).
    • Non-destructive, with Reset.
    • Saved-clip audio decodes with AudioDecoder where it exists, otherwise the WASM decoder, then decodeAudioData.
  • My Clips (/clips): guest-visible. Filter chips: All, Kept, Auto, Pictures, Links, and each game. Sections Today / This week / Earlier. The clip tile is the one signature layout primitive, reused everywhere. A storage line with Tidy up.
  • Home and profile:
    • A My Clips shelf and profile section.
    • Three separate name controls, each writing one field (a test proves independence): the profile toggle says only "Show my gamer name on leaderboards" (the field has no UI today); Clip settings "Name on my clips" is the local default; the Style tab changes one clip.
    • Guests see "No name", with no sign-in nudge (signup is #26i).
  • Trophies: Director! (first clip), Film Crew! (10 clips), Slow-Mo Master! (first slow-mo). No trophy for sharing or making links.
  • First run:
    • One start-card hint line for the first 3 sessions per game, also on four-wheeler-3d's StartScreen and the Retro Arcade picker (both register with useStartOverlayPresence). A contract test checks every start surface.
    • The coach mark shows on the first run of 20 s or longer with no auto clip: "Next time something cool happens, tap 🎬!". At a first new best, only the highlight chip shows. A test ensures the two never share a break.

11.5 Auto-highlights

  • markMoment({ kind, label, emoji, priority, offsetSec }) with negative offsets (Steam Timeline model), plus runPhase('start'|'end'). For zero-code games, a best-score change or a GameStartOverlay remount counts as run end.
  • Featured moments auto-clip 8 s before to 3 s after, at most one per 45 s and six per session. Standard moments are filmstrip stars.
  • Anti-nag:
    • At most one highlight chip per run, and one per 3 minutes.
    • Three ignored chips mute chips for that game for 7 days; clips still land in Auto.
    • A manual clip covering the moment suppresses the duplicate.
    • A manual clip's own card at the break is exempt from the chip cap.
  • Zero-code fallback: the achievements personal-best watermark gives every game a game-over-anchored "new best" highlight until the game adopts markMoment.

11.6 Copy rules (phrase-level)

  • The copy test runs against the real string table: "save" may appear only in the "Save to ..." phrases, and there are no sign-in prompts on clip surfaces.
  • No em-dashes in shipped copy.
  • Every error has a kid-word reason and a next step.
  • Emoji paired with a word on nav links and chips are a pre-reader aid, but headings stay plain text. This is a proposal (decision 11).

12. Sharing

PlatformPrimaryAlso offered
iPhone / iPad Safari, Home Screen appShare (share sheet), with "Save Video" coaching for the first 3 sharesMake a link (Phase 7). "Save to Files" only above the measured share ceiling.
Chrome for iOS, in-app browsers on iOSShare where canShare says yes, else "Open in Safari"Make a link
Android Chrome / Samsung (32-bit Chrome shares WebM)ShareSave to phone, Make a link
Android WebView in-app browsers"Open in Chrome" handoffMake a link

  • Share calls navigator.share({ files: [file] }) with files only, synchronously in the tap. The file is typed per tier (video/mp4 with .mp4, or video/webm with .webm). Names are <host-slug>-<game>-<yyyymmdd-hhmm>, built from the deployment's own origin.
  • Results:
    • Shared: a celebration.
    • AbortError: "No problem. Your clip is safe in My Clips."
    • NotAllowedError: re-arm with "Tap Share one more time."
    • InvalidStateError (double tap): ignore it.
    • Type rejection: fall back to Save to phone or Save to computer.
    • Screen Time or Family Link blocks: a kid-word reason, never silence.
    Failures log a values-free reason.
  • The game pauses before the share sheet opens, where it can pause.
  • Without Phase 7, desktop and Chromebook kids have Save to computer and the OS share only (stated in decision 1).

13. Hosted clip links (Phase 7, if approved)

The only part with running cost and legal weight. It ships last, behind its own flag, and fails closed in code: the grant route returns 503 unless attestation variables are set (DMCA agent registration number, NCMEC ESP id, a non-empty moderator allowlist, privacy notice version). A kid's clone with only a bucket configured cannot make public links.

13.1 Serving and cost

  • Bucket: hanks-hits-clips in the sidecar storage project, pinned to one region (Tigris Global placement is eventually consistent, so takedowns would not be immediate). A separate legal-hold bucket's credentials live only in the admin legal-hold action.
  • Upload: a presigned PUT signs x-amz-checksum-sha256, Content-Type and Content-Length, which binds the exact bytes. Video bytes never pass through the Cloudflare-fronted site. The one exception is the capped school fallback below.
  • Views: /c/<slug>/v.mp4 answers 302 to a presigned GET with Cache-Control: no-store. Bucket egress is free.
  • Origin lock (only when CLIPS_ORIGIN_SECRET is set, so local dev and kid clones are unaffected): a Cloudflare Transform Rule adds the secret header, and middleware rejects /c/* and /api/clips/* without it.
  • Breaker: a Postgres /c/* request-and-byte breaker serves a tiny static fallback when over budget and alerts. Railway's soft usage alert is set, never a hard limit.
  • Page: a Route Handler returns hand-built HTML (Next 16 streams metadata into the body for user agents it does not list, and Cloudflare caches HTML without the user agent).
    • Headers: the global '/:path*' rule gets a negative lookahead excluding /c/. /c/:slug, /c/:slug/embed and /c/:slug/v.mp4 each re-declare the full set: HSTS, nosniff, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy (camera, microphone, display-capture, geolocation off) and exactly one CSP, whose media-src names the bucket host and the school fallback host. The embed has no X-Frame-Options, and its frame-ancestors list the unfurl hosts. Header tests assert all of it.
    • The video source is the stable /c/<slug>/v.mp4, and it refreshes on a media error.
    • Slugs carry at least 96 bits of entropy behind a rate-limited 404 path.
  • Sizes: link copies are at most 9 MB for inline iMessage play. The client re-encodes with the game paused, targeting 92% of budget and retrying lower at most twice before offering a shorter trim. Tier V's size fit happens in the server transcode. Long videos up to 50 MiB get tap-to-play links. The link job resumes after the kid returns from another app, and the sweeper finalizes server-side when all objects landed.
  • School fallback: a separate route family, /s/<token>, on a named grey-cloud (DNS-only) host, used only after a direct bucket load fails.
    • Authenticated by a short-lived HMAC token from the Cloudflare-fronted page, not the edge header.
    • Range support; getClientIP uses x-real-ip only there.
    • Per-identity and global byte caps (20 GB/month by default).
    • When capped, the page says "This video can't play on this network. Try at home!"
Monthly1k links10k linksCeiling of default cap (about 37k)Worst-case abuse
Bucket storage (9.6 MB blended link, 30-day life, factor 1.12; Send-to-a-phone objects live 48 h and round to $0)$0.17$1.62$6.00$6.00 (committed-bytes cap)
Service egress: HTML and 302s (20 views + 5 unfurls per link)$0.02$0.21$0.78$5 (the /c breaker setting)
School fallback egress ($0.05/GB, 20 GB cap)$0-1$0-1$0-1$1
Screener CPU (10-80 vCPU-s per standard link, 10x for the 5% long links, $0.00000772/vCPU-s; concurrency 1 at a 1 vCPU cap)$0.11-0.90$1.10-9.00$4.10-20.30$20.30 (1 vCPU x 730 h)
Screener RAM (0.5-1.5 GB always on, sweeper inside, $10/GB-month)$5-15$5-15$5-15$15 (1.5 GB cgroup cap)
Video delivery$0$0$0$0
Totalabout $5-17about $8-26about $16-43about $47

The per-link CPU range covers every screening stage: full-frame decode at 2-4 fps plus scene changes, faces, nudity, speech, QR, scene text, game-likeness embeddings, and the WebM transcode. The Phase 7 staging gate measures p50/p95 vCPU-s and RSS on 200 real clips, and the table is rebuilt from those numbers.

  • At the high estimate, concurrency 1 screens about 1,000 links a day. Above that, concurrency goes to 2, which doubles the CPU ceiling to about $41. That is a measured decision.
  • Past about 37k new links a month, the 400 GB committed-bytes cap binds. A Pushover alert fires at 70%, and raising the cap is a documented step with its cost (1.1 TB is about $16.50/month).
  • The research reports the workspace already appears to be on Railway Pro; confirm. Hobby caps all buckets at 1 TB combined.

13.2 Abuse and moderation

  • Validation before live:
    • HEAD each object.
    • Box-walk the MP4: fast-start, one avc track and at most one aac track, no udta, caps.
    • Or walk the WebM EBML for Tier V; the screener then transcodes it.
    • Check the JPEGs, and re-hash the video.
  • Screening (open models, always on). Every frame is decoded, sampled at 2-4 fps plus scene changes. HOLD signals:
    • Faces (YuNet), nudity (OpenNSFW2), speech (Silero VAD).
    • QR codes and barcodes (OpenCV).
    • Scene text (an ONNX detector) outside declared text regions:
      • The compositor declares the rectangles it painted per frame (result card, end card, game-name band) in a signed sidecar, and the screener verifies them against the band layout.
      • Games declare dynamic text boxes from their draw calls, or glyph sets such as digits for 2048 and Math Attack.
      • World signs are registered per game.
    • Game-likeness: frame embeddings near the claimed game's real calibration corpus. Below threshold, or a better match for another game, means HOLD. This also checks the client-declared gameId, so per-game policy cannot be bypassed.
    Each detector is calibrated per game on real clips before its HOLD goes live. ffmpeg runs hardened: pipe-only, an h264/aac/vp8/opus decoder allowlist, non-root, no network, cgroup limits.
  • Session-blind until #26i is fixed (PR 7.0 fixes it first): hosted routes never read the account session while signup collects a child's email without consent. The flow runs purely on 312.5(c)(7) (device cookie and IP). There is no handle snapshot, no account quota key, no account ownership, and no account-synced list.
  • Page text is fixed: the game name and "a player on <host>" from the deployment origin. No score in page text. A source-scan test bans the literals "hankshits" and "Hank's Hits" under shared/clips and the /c route.
  • Identity and caps (all numbers are defaults, editable in the DB settings row):
    • Client IP: the fixed getClientIP (#29i).
    • Hierarchical keys: IPv6 /64, /56 and /48; IPv4 /24 with a school-NAT allowance; ASN from the public-domain iptoasn dataset. A stable reputation HMAC sits beside a daily-rotated counting HMAC.
    • Established identity: a device cookie at least 7 days old with no upheld reports.
    • Unestablished pool: 1,500 standard plus 50 long links a day. Unestablished grants carry a 1-2 s client proof-of-work, a 7-day link life and no renew.
    • Established pool: 3,500 plus 100 a day. Links live 30 days, renewable to 90.
    • Per identity: 6 an hour, 20 a day, 2 long a day.
    • Every screened upload counts, rejected ones included.
    • The screening queue is fair per identity, with established identities first. A timeout retries rather than holding.
    • Abuse test: saturate the unestablished pool, then assert that an established identity still gets a grant.
  • Reports:
    • The kid report is structured reason chips only, with no text box and no contact field. DMCA and removal requests are separate grown-up forms under /legal, and their contact details are deleted when resolved.
    • "Real person", "private info" and "intimate imagery" reports hide the clip at once.
    • A clean-scoring clip reported only by unestablished reporters is restored after 24 h, unless an established reporter or an admin agrees. Intimate-imagery reports stay hidden until reviewed.
    • Review time: 48 h (proposal); a named backup reviewer; a volume alarm; a review-age alarm at 48 h.
    • The owner sees "A grown-up is checking this one." A hold that expires unreviewed tells the kid in My Clips, and the local clip is untouched.
    • Blocklists (SHA-256 and perceptual hashes) are added only after human review, with per-game common-frame exclusions.
    • Takedowns 404 at the edge within 5 minutes.
  • Admin /admin/clips: allowlist plus TOTP, re-auth within 12 hours, audit log, frames blurred until clicked. Pushover alerts carry values only (clip id, reason code, counts, console link); a unit test checks the payload.
  • Per-game hosting policy (a literal, fail closed):
    • Retro Arcade: off (Railway's Acceptable Use Policy on copyrighted media is workspace-wide).
    • Apps: off.
    • Kid-built or kid-changed modules: on only with a current scrub hash and a zero-hold calibration run (the module's __clips__/drive.ts makes N clips headlessly).

13.3 Link experience

  • Sender: "Make a link", then a quick check ("No real names, schools, or towns in your clip?"), then "Sending your clip..." with kid-word states (sending, checking, ready, "a grown-up is checking this one").
    • When ready: Share (a fresh tap), Copy link, a QR code, and "Works until <date>". The date comes from the grant's real expires_at, so the 7-day and 30-day lives both show honestly. One source feeds the copy, the recipient countdown, the renew route and the notice.
    • The link is written to the current owner partition. "My links" lists from GET /api/clips/mine (device cookie), filtered to the link ids that partition knows, so siblings on one device see only their own.
    • The Home Screen app has its own cookie jar, and the eviction copy says so.
  • Recipient page:
    • A muted inline autoplay loop with a big tap-to-unmute.
    • A vanilla speechSynthesis read-aloud button (allowed by a CSP hash).
    • "This video goes away in N days".
    • "Play this game, free".
    • An "Open in Chrome" handoff for Android in-app browsers.
    • A report link.
    • noindex.
    • A 410 gone state with its own read-aloud.
  • Send to a phone: a hosted object type on the same grant, validator, screener, caps and sweeper, with a 48-hour life. It has a minimal noindex landing page ("Get the video", then navigator.share for Save Video). Any number of Range requests is allowed within the window. PR 7.5.
  • Owner-only reactions (decision 4d, PR 7.6): preset emoji only, counts visible only to the owner, no free text, device-cookie keyed.

13.4 Legal checklist (the flag stays off until every item is done)

  • #26i fixed by PR 7.0 (signup rework per decision 8, plus a reviewed production write that deletes or consents existing child emails, with before and after counts).
  • COPPA 312.4(d) notice at /privacy (base in PR 0.3, clips section in PR 7.3). Linked from the Make-a-link sheet, the report form and every /c/ footer. It includes the 312.4(d)(3) internal-operations statement for the device cookie, IP and reputation keys.
  • 312.10 written retention policy, every item with a purpose and a deletion time:
    • Established links: 30 days, renewable to 90. Unestablished links: 7 days.
    • Send-to-a-phone transfers: 48 hours.
    • Face, voice and text holds: 7 days unreviewed, or at once on confirmation of personal information.
    • Nudity holds: kept until a human reviews them (review-age alarm at 48 h), then deleted, or moved to a 1-year legal hold if reported to NCMEC.
    • Counting HMACs: 48 hours.
    • Reputation HMAC and strike records: 12 months after the last event.
    • Device cookie: 1 year.
    • IP ciphertext: deleted with the clip, or 1 year under a legal hold.
    • Report records without contact details: 12 months.
    • Postgres PITR window: stated.
    • Edge caches: 5 minutes.
    • A statement that copies inside recipients' apps are outside our control.
  • 312.8(b) written information-security program (coordinator Jack, annual risk assessment); 312.8(c) assurances from Railway, Cloudflare and Pushover.
  • Mailing address: the Copyright Office PO-box waiver (poboxwaiver@copyright.gov, citing child safety). The DMCA designation and /privacy use the non-home address, phone and email from Phase 0. Never a home address.
  • DMCA 512(c): designated agent ($6, 3-year renewal), notice and counter-notice forms, repeat-infringer policy.
  • 18 U.S.C. 2258A: NCMEC CyberTipline ESP registration; written preservation procedure.
  • A no-account removal form (TAKE IT DOWN Act practice; the site plausibly falls under its "preselected content" exclusion).
  • UK: a short DPIA; an Online Safety Act "links with the UK" assessment; an EU DSA applicability note. Re-check when UK or EU traffic passes a stated threshold.
  • Counsel check: "operator for commercial purposes"; state minor-social-media laws.

14. Kid-built games and apps

  • Every module has an explicit clips literal. The coverage test fails on any GameShell appId without one.
    • Games: on.
    • Trivia, Drum Machine, Virtual Pet: on (DOM replay).
    • drawing-app: off (free drawing).
    • weather, joke-generator, toy-finder, profile, leaderboards: off (no gameplay).
    Every "off" carries a reason comment.
  • Kid-built and kid-changed modules clip only with a current scrub hash (section 10). Canvas games need no clip code. DOM games get a scaffolded lib/clipRenderer.ts and __clips__/drive.ts. The make-a-game scaffold uses useGameLoop for kid-built 2D canvas games (not R3F or iframe games).
  • Siblings first: the games make-a-game tells Claude to copy migrate first, so every copy inherits a working clip source, renderer and audio channel. change-a-game and remix-a-game add "re-verify clip parity" and "re-run the scrub".
  • Edits land with the code they describe:
    • PR 1.1a: the audio lint rule, the CLAUDE.md checklist line, and the make-a-game, change-a-game and remix-a-game audio edits.
    • PR 2.1: the metadata parser for clips, clipsScrubbed, kidChanged and hosting literals, with tests.
    • PR 2.10: the scrub writer (hash), the skill edits that run it, the put-it-online extension to canvas and clipRenderer text, the literal scan, and auto-discovery.
    • Phase 4: renderer scaffolding.
    • Phase 6: kid-facing clip button nuggets in play-my-game and getting-started.
    • mobile-playability additions: the header fits at 320-480 px in all states; no control sits under the header or toast slot; the clip button works by tap; Record is reachable by visible taps; trim handles are 48x96 px with tap-a-frame; Leaderboard is reachable at game over.

15. Verification plan

"Works" is decided on real phones and on hankshits.com. Every file from every device is pulled back to this Mac and judged by ffmpeg/ffprobe and a Swift AVFoundation checker (what iPhone Photos and Messages use).

15.1 Tiers

TierWhatRuns
Unit (vitest)
  • Engine: rings; timeline slots at 30/41/50/60/75/90/120/144/165 Hz (a 41 fps irregular trace gives video length equal to audio within 1 frame); clock map and PLL; mixer watermarks; resampler; ASC builder; roll-group box writer; epoch classification.
  • Control and storage: governor traces; owner purge rules including the 60 s sign-in window; library protocol; share decision table.
  • UX rules: copy table rules; handle never in live frames; independent name controls; tap semantics (a hold leaves no clip; 5 taps in 10 s give one row; tap, tap within 5 s, tap after 6 s give two rows; no viewer opens during play).
  • Hosted: the Pushover payload builder; the scrub hash gate; a sweeper test (a nudity hold older than 7 days is not deleted).
  • Release: the production default flag after release (a production build with no env shows the clip button).

15.2 Pass thresholds

  • Container A/V on synthetic-timestamp files: within 5 ms in BOTH ffmpeg and AVFoundation, at every beep, for every encoder path. Decoders that ignore edit lists: at most +45 ms.
  • Live end-to-end A/V: inside ITU-R BT.1359 (+45 / -125 ms) at every beep over the whole clip, including a Bluetooth route.
  • Frame rate:
    • Game frame-rate loss with capture on: at most 10%, enforced at runtime, at the rung Phase 0 predicts.
    • Effective capture fps at least 90% of the settled rung on desktop and 80% on phones.
    • Largest gap at most 100 ms outside pauses.
  • Time to first encoded frame: at most 1000 ms p95, on a normally launched browser.
  • Memory: growth at most 50 MB over 10 minutes always-on; no memory kill on the oldest supported iPhone in a 15-minute heavy-game soak; exports stay inside the per-class budget (6.5).
  • Clip build: a 30 s clip builds in at most 0.5 s on the reference phone; share-ready at most 2 s.
  • Color: delta-E 2000 at most 3 on the bars, per encoder family.
  • Header: right edge inside the viewport and title at least 16 px across the 11.2 matrix, in all three states.
  • Phase 1: per-game speed targets asserted in loop tests at 60/90/120/144 Hz; no p95 frame-time regression over 10% on a low-end Android and a Chromebook.

15.3 Harness and gates

  • Harness files:
    • e2e/clips/playwright.config.ts, e2e/clips/lib/* and e2e/clips/*.spec.ts.
    • tools/clips/{avcheck,avsync}.swift.
    • scripts/clips/{lab-server,tunnel,android-device,ios-device,ios-sim,safari-desktop,matrix}.mjs.
  • Clips lab route: a dynamic server component that 404s unless CLIPS_LAB=1 at runtime (a privacy test asserts prod 404s). Tunnels add a per-session token path, and device sessions use a local Docker Postgres with seeded users, never production.
  • Pre-push: the gate probes for tools (Playwright browsers, branded Chrome, ffprobe, swiftc on darwin). It prints one SKIPPED row per missing tool and never fails on a missing tool, so kids' Windows and Chromebook clones push normally. Changes under shared/clips or GameShell require clips:matrix by hand before merge. The gate's measured duration is recorded here after PR 0.6.
  • Release gates: they run against the Docker image started the way the Dockerfile CMD does.
  • Weekly Chrome Beta, Safari and EmulatorJS-release runs: a local launchd job on Jack's Mac with Pushover alerts, never GitHub Actions.

16. Phases and pull requests

Every PR follows the same gates:

  • The local CI gate from .githooks/pre-push, a /dcr review sized to the change, /qa and mobile-playability for UI, and a live smoke after deploy.
  • Integration PRs carry at most 2-3 modules each. Phase 1 class-fix PRs apply one mechanical change per game with shared tests, split into waves of at most 7 games; that is the named exception, because a class fix split by game would duplicate its tests and review.
  • Each PR converges by the /dcr rule, and a third wave that still finds branch-introduced defects means split the PR.
  • Game fixes ship live as they merge. Clip UI stays behind CLIPS_MODE until Phase 6.
  • Rollback: redeploy the previous Railway deployment, then git revert.

Phase 0: truth spike and prerequisites (about 140 h)

  • PR 0.1:
    • Next and eslint-config-next to 16.3.x.
    • Dockerfile from Node 20 (end of life 2026-04-30) to the current Node LTS, with the same major pinned locally.
    • Check whether 16.3 next dev writes AGENTS.md or CLAUDE.md, and disable it if so.
    • Verify sign-in, progress, leaderboards and the emulator route; live smoke.
  • PR 0.2: pin EmulatorJS 4.2.3 (#22i) with a pin test; display-capture=() with a header test.
  • PR 0.3: /privacy COPPA notice for what the site collects today, with the non-home address, phone and email from Jack. Linked from the footer and signup.
  • PR 0.4: fix getClientIP behind Cloudflare after a values-free live check (#29i); apply it to the signup, progress and ROM limiters.
  • PR 0.5: WASM AAC encoder and decoder build (script, SIMD and scalar .wasm, FFmpeg source tarball and configure line under public/licenses, a reproducibility check).
  • PR 0.6: the clips lab (runtime-gated; ports design/clips/prototype/; pulls forward only a capabilities probe and a minimal encode and mux loop), the validators, @playwright/test, and pinned mediabunny. It measures:
    • Compositor paths A-D.
    • Encoder: TTFC per latencyMode, keyframe response and the forced-session fallback, portrait support, whether WebKit accepts oversized frames, concurrent encoder sessions on Android.
    • Audio: AAC delay per encoder, W+ with AudioData absent, and the iPhone ringer.
    • Storage and sharing: OPFS and persist() per surface, the iOS share ceiling, export memory, session-timeline flash writes and battery.
    • Quality: color bars, 60 fps readback.
    • Firefox: VP8 keyframe spacing and the rotation period.
    • It re-runs every "proven" row on Chrome 155 and Safari/iOS 27.
  • Device spike on every inventory device. Append the decision memo.

Phase 1: shared game foundations (about 300 h, ships live)

  • PR 1.1a: getGameAudio() (speaker gain after the tap point, limiter, document-level unlock, pass-through iframe shim, no tap yet), the ESLint ban, the source-scan test, and the CLAUDE.md and skill audio edits.
  • PR 1.1b-d: migrate the audio sites (canvas games; monster-truck and four-wheeler-3d; apps and iframe realms). Listen tests through the limiter. iPhone smoke: drum-machine and virtual-pet sound on the first tap.
  • PR 1.2a-c: shared useGameLoop:
    • Fixed step with alpha interpolation and a dt clamp.
    • Migrate every game whose loop re-creates each frame.
    • The speed table from decision 3 is asserted at 60/90/120/144 Hz. If decision 3 restores the designed speed, pre-change bests are marked "old speed" on the leaderboard, or those four boards reset with a notice.
    • Stop the per-frame storage writes.
  • PR 1.3a-b:
    • Pointer events instead of onClick plus onTouchStart, including Monster Truck's pedals with touch-action: none (closes #18i).
    • Restart grace on every result card.
    • bestAtRunStart snapshots, including Monster Truck's airtime and flip records and Four-Wheeler Adventure's per-vehicle race best.
    • The shared result chip with read-aloud, Leaderboard and Restart.
    • #30i, #31i, #33i, #34i.
    • Board-game AI moves no longer land under the pause menu (chess, checkers, quoridor get an onPause).
  • PR 1.4a-c: real sound effects for silent modules, including Monster Truck's engine and music, and every sound toggle wired to the speaker gain or deleted (#24i).

    • ToastLane.

Sum: about 2,100 hours (about 1,670 without Phase 7), ±30%, across about 58 PRs. Contingency: if Phase 0 selects path B or C for 3D games on iPhone, add about 40 h to Phase 3. Claude does the building; the pacing constraint is Jack's device sessions, grants and merges.
