It is Hank's own 120x120 2D dial (:430, drawn at :5381-5394), copied pixel-exact. - #raceStatus when style.display === 'block': a top-center pill with its text ('🟢 You lead!', item flashes, '🏆 YOU WIN! +$10,000 🏆'). - #countdown when style.display === 'flex': huge center text ('3', '2', '1', 'GO!') with Hank's look (:84-89): white bold, 0 6px 0 #14492a shadow. - #rainbowBanner (:505, shown at :2189) when visible: its two-line text. STALE-CANVAS RISK: none. draw() runs every frame, even behind panels (:5399-5405). END CARD: bones `${collected}/${total}`, cash, skulls, and on a race win the time plus 'NEW BEST' for that vehicle label (VLABEL, index.html:901).

Clip layout

ORIENTATION: output orientation is chosen once, at session start, from the iframe viewport aspect. - Landscape 1280x720; portrait 720x1280. - The adventure has no OrientationWarning and plays in portrait with its absolute-positioned touch buttons. - A later rotation is contained into the fixed output with bars. A clip can never span two encoder sizes. SCALING: smooth, high quality. canvas#game is window x DPR capped at 2 (index.html:663-669), so this is a downscale of 2D vector art. CROP: none. The iframe already starts below the header (Game.tsx:88 top-12/md:top-14). FIT: auto (the same ±20% cover rule as the 3D games). RATE: 30 fps, 3 Mbps hint (flat 2D art compresses well). RESIZE: canvas#game resizes on window resize (index.html:668). With a fixed compositor this only changes the source rect. The feasibility study's 'restart recording on resize' gotcha does not apply to this design.

Brag events

MECHANISM: the adapter polls __hhProbe() once per tick and edge-detects against the previous snapshot. After every (re)load, the FIRST snapshot is the baseline and emits nothing, so a restart or first load never fires a brag. The events: 1) RACE WIN (priority 3). Transition prev.raceState === 'racing' to now 'done' with winner === 'you' (index.html:2106-2115). race.time is frozen once the state is 'done' (:2082-2083), so timeMs = round(raceTime * 1000). - Per-vehicle personal best in a wrapper-owned, device-owned key fwa_race_best_v1 = {[raceCar]: ms}. Device-owned is consistent with Hank's fwa_myland_v1 (shared/lib/localProgress.ts:47-50), which is deliberately not cleared on sign-out. - newBest = no stored value || timeMs < stored. Read the stored value BEFORE writing. - Emit {type:'win', timeMs, vehicle: VLABEL, newBest, prevBestMs}, plus newHighScore {better:'lower'} when improving an existing best. - Do NOT add this key to DEVICE_OWNED_ALIASES; that would change the shelf stat source. 2) ALL BONES (priority 3). prev.collected < total && now.collected === total && total > 0 (the :2010 condition). 3) WORLD RECORD SKULLS (priority 3). now.skulls > max(312, peakEmitted), then set peakEmitted = now.skulls (the WR badge rule at :2151). mountedSkulls resets at :5150 and :5569, so the peak is per iframe session. 4) RAINBOW FISH (priority 3). prev.rainbowTimer <= 0 && now.rainbowTimer > 0 (set to 6 at :6107). 5) MARKERS (priority 1): each bone pickup (collected increments) and each race start (idle to countdown). NO CASINO CLIPS: casino wins (index.html:5881-5886) are deliberately NOT auto-clipped, so the brag system does not promote gambling-style wins. RELIABILITY: MutationObservers on the badges were considered and rejected. Hank rewrites #skulls and #record textContent every update() (:2150-2151), which queues a childList mutation every frame even with no change. Numeric probe polling is cheaper and exact.

Audio

CURRENT STATE: one AudioContext created in startAudio() (index.html:1705-1711). A master gain (:1710-1711) carries only the engine synth. The one-shots boom (:1631), bow (:5182), gunshot (:5195) and grunt (:6044) connect straight to audioCtx.destination, so a tap on master alone misses them. FIX, without touching Hank's file: the prelude's HHAudioContext shadows the instance's destination with a bus GainNode that feeds the real destination. Every existing connect() then lands on bus. TAP: the adapter calls kit.attachForeignTap(win.__hhAudio.bus, win). Inside that realm the kit does: - ctx.audioWorklet.addModule(<same-origin tap URL>) - new win.AudioWorkletNode(ctx, 'hh-clip-tap'), using the realm's own constructor - bus.connect(tapNode), with no output to destination - transfer tapNode.port to the encoder worker - clock mapping via ctx.getOutputTimestamp() plus the timeOrigin delta (Safari 14.1+, per mdn/browser-compat-data api/AudioContext.json) Not needed: engineAudio is null (:1697, 1740-1741), and the file loads no external media (pinned by __tests__/no-external-media.test.ts). SOUNDS TO ADD: the brag moments are silent today. Race win, all bones, rainbow fish and the world-record skull make no sound. The wrapper plays short synthesized celebrations on the SAME edges, built from win.__hhAudio.ctx nodes into bus. The player hears them live and the clip records them: - win: 6-note triangle fanfare - bone: 2-note sine blip at gain 0.15 on every pickup - all-bones: arpeggio - rainbow: a sparkle glissando - WR: fanfare They live in games/four-wheeler-adventure/clip/celebrations.ts and are skipped when __hhAudio is missing (startAudio failed at :1734). There is no mute toggle in this game, so they follow the game's audio state.

Registration

PIN FIRST (#22i). public/emulator/index.html:169 EJS_pathtodata and :239 loader.js both point at https://cdn.emulatorjs.org/stable/data/. Change both to https://cdn.emulatorjs.org/4.2.3/data/. Every hook below uses EJS internals: canvas, Module.AL, gameManager.getVideoDimensions, paused and settingsMenuOpen. EMULATOR PAGE PRELUDE: add a new inline <script> at the very top of <body> in public/emulator/index.html, before the script at :77. It MUST run before loader.js loads EJS. The meta CSP at :7-15 and the /emulator route CSP (next.config.ts:41-54) allow inline scripts. - Wrap drawArrays, drawElements, drawArraysInstanced, drawElementsInstanced, drawRangeElements and clear on BOTH WebGLRenderingContext.prototype and WebGL2RenderingContext.prototype. - Track bindFramebuffer per context, so a draw only counts when the DEFAULT framebuffer is bound. - On the first such draw in a task, add this.canvas to a dirty set and queueMicrotask(flush). flush calls window.__hhClip.sink(performance.now()) if a sink is set. - WHY: the EJS canvas is WebGL with preserveDrawingBuffer:false. The spec presents only after a draw and clears the buffer after compositing (registry.khronos.org/webgl/specs/latest/1.0/, section 2.2), so a read in any other task, or in a rAF where RetroArch drew nothing, returns black. - WHY A MICROTASK: it runs at the end of the drawing task and before compositing, whether Emscripten's main loop uses rAF or setTimeout. A rAF registration-order hack would not survive a setTimeout main loop. PARENT REGISTRATION, inside EmulatorView's message handler (games/retro-arcade/Game.tsx:209-264), at the 'ready' branch (:213): - First add `if (event.source !== iframeRef.current?.contentWindow) return;`. Today only the origin is checked (:211), so any same-origin window could fake a ready. - Then run `disposeRef.current?.(); disposeRef.current = registerRetroClipSource(iframeRef.current!, {system, title: romUrl.startsWith('blob:') ? null : romName, restoreFocus})`. The adapter lives at games/retro-arcade/clip/source.ts. An uploaded ROM's file name (a blob: URL, Game.tsx:72-83) never reaches a clip. ADAPTER (runs after EJS_onGameStart, emulator/index.html:205-208): - Take ejs = win.EJS_emulator. - Contract check: ejs.canvas is a canvas, ejs.gameManager.getVideoDimensions is a function, ejs.Module.AL.currentCtx.gain is an instanceof win.AudioNode. On failure, mark clips unavailable for this session with a values-free reason code, not a crash. - Set win.__hhClip.sink = ts => kit.pushFrame(convertTs(ts), drawNative). DISPOSAL: dispose on the next 'ready', on the effect cleanup, and on EmulatorView unmount. Restart remounts via key={store.restartNonce} (Game.tsx:375) and exit via stopGame (:365-367) unmounts; both produce a fresh iframe, a fresh 'ready' and a re-register. ROUTE: add appId="retro-arcade" to <GameShell> in app/games/retro-arcade/page.tsx:28-32. It passes none today. There is no leaderboard extractor for this game, so no leaderboard button appears. What the compositor paints

HUD: none needed. The emulated game draws its own score, lives and timer into the frame. The EJS toolbar, virtual gamepad and menus are DOM and are correctly excluded. COMPOSITOR PIPELINE: (1) Crop the picture out of the EJS canvas, computed per capture: - a = getVideoDimensions('aspect'), nw/nh = getVideoDimensions('width'/'height'). These are cwrap('get_video_dimensions') calls in EJS 4.2.3 and cost almost nothing. - For a canvas W×H: if W/H > a, the picture is vw = H·a, x0 = (W − vw)/2, y0 = 0. - Otherwise vw = W, vh = W/a, x0 = 0, and y0 = 0, i.e. TOP-aligned. EJS writes video_top_portrait_viewport = true into retroarch.cfg (getRetroArchCfg in emulator.min.js 4.2.3). EJS's own screenRecord centers it (ejs-emulator.js:6100-6108), which is wrong in portrait. (2) Native recovery: drawImage(crop → an nw×nh 'native' canvas, imageSmoothingEnabled=false). EJS sets video_smooth = false, so the canvas is a nearest-neighbor upscale and sampling at pixel centers recovers the exact native frame at any DPR, with tolerance ±s/2 px. (3) The native canvas is updated only on dirty captures. The encode tick paints the last native frame, so the output holds the last frame and never goes black. (4) Pillarbox bars: flat brand panels. Left: 'Retro Arcade' plus the console name from SYSTEMS[system].name. Right: the catalog title (null for uploads) plus the handle. The bottom-right bar carries hankshits.com. No overlay ever covers the game picture, because retro HUDs often sit at the frame edges. END CARD: console, the catalog title if known, the handle. IDLE: ejs.paused || ejs.settingsMenuOpen || ejs.disksMenuOpen (the same flags EJS checks at ejs-emulator.js:1721), plus the loading screen before 'ready'. A paused main loop issues no draws, so the dirty gate also stops frames naturally.

Clip layout

OUTPUT: 1280x720 with the game picture pixel-perfect in the center. SCALE: vertical integer scale k = floor(720 / nh). Width = round(k · nh · aspect) to an even number. Upscale native → output with imageSmoothingEnabled=false. PER CORE: - SNES and Genesis 224 lines: k=3, 896x672 (SNES 8:7 pixel aspect) or 960x672. - NES 240 lines: 960x720. - Game Boy 144 lines: k=5, 800x720. - GBA 160 lines: k=4, 960x640. - Atari 2600: whatever EJS reports; the same integer rule applies. Hi-res modes: SNES 512/448 lines, or N64 variable resolution. When nh changes mid-game, recompute k and contain into the fixed 1280x720 output. The encoder never restarts. FRAME RATE: 60 fps, not 30. The sink fires once per emulated frame, so capture follows the core's native 60 Hz and only decimates above 60 (for example a 120 Hz rAF). Scrolling shooters and platformers look choppy at 30. BITRATE: 3 Mbps hint. Flat palettes compress well, but the sharp nearest-neighbor edges need bits.

Brag events

There is no automatic brag detection, and that is an honest, inherent limit. The emulator is a black box. EJS 4.2.3 exposes no memory-read API: its cwrap surface is cmd_savefiles, cmd_take_screenshot, get_video_dimensions, get_current_frame_count, load_state, save_state_info, set_cheat, simulate_input, system_restart and similar, with no retro_get_memory_data. It also has no RetroAchievements integration. Per-title score hooks would need a rebuilt core. SUPPORTED: - Manual 'Clip it!' from the Steam-style instant-replay ring, for the last 15, 30 or 60 s. - Manual Record. - Markers at 'ready' (game start). NOT MARKERS: EJS save-state events (Game.tsx:233-242) are housekeeping.

Audio

SOURCE: EmulatorJS plays through Emscripten OpenAL in its OWN AudioContext inside the iframe realm. Keep that playback path untouched (zero-risk). TAP POINT: Module.AL.currentCtx.gain. In Emscripten's OpenAL, alcCreateContext makes a context gain connected to ac.destination (github.com/emscripten-core/emscripten/blob/main/src/lib/libopenal.js, `var gain = ac.createGain(); gain.connect(ac.destination)`, ~:2156-2157). EVERY source's gain connects to AL.currentCtx.gain (alGenSources, ~:2760-2761). So tapping ctx.gain catches all current AND future sources, post EJS volume slider. The slider sets source gains (ejs-emulator.js:2039-2043), so a muted emulator records silence, which is what the kid heard. WHY NOT EJS's HELPER: this is better than EJS's collectScreenRecordingMediaTracks (ejs-emulator.js:6031-6071). That helper merges only the sources that exist at call time, through a ChannelMerger that downmixes each source to one channel. TAP MECHANICS: kit.attachForeignTap(AL.currentCtx.gain, win). The worklet module must be a same-origin URL. The /emulator CSP script-src includes 'self' and blob:, but see groupNotes for the main-route rule. Build the node with win.AudioWorkletNode and transfer its port to the encoder worker. Map the clock with getOutputTimestamp() plus the timeOrigin delta. RESUME: Emscripten auto-resumes on keydown, mousedown or touchstart (libcore.js autoResumeAudioContext, ~:1817-1825), and EJS shows a 'Click to resume Emulator' popup (ejs-emulator.js:1038-1055). While the context is suspended the tap is silent and the kit zero-fills. NO SFX TO ADD: the games have their own audio.

Replay log and renderer

n/a. This is live WebGL capture. Libretro cores are deterministic given a state plus an input log, but EJS 4.2.3 has no input-movie API. A viewer would also need the ROM, which is a non-starter for sharing.

EJS_Buttons hides screenRecord and screenshot. 2) crop.test.ts, pure: landscape centered (2400x1616 at 4:3), portrait TOP-aligned (1170x2100), exact fit, SNES 8:7, GB 10:9, GBA 3:2. Native-recovery sample points fall inside source blocks for scale factors 1.0 to 6.0. 3) integerLayout.test.ts: the per-core output rect is even, fits in 1280x720, and is centered. 4) prelude.test.ts, running the prelude against fake WebGL prototypes: - a draw on the default framebuffer marks the canvas dirty and schedules exactly one microtask per task - FBO-bound draws do not - the sink fires after the task and not otherwise 5) adapter lifecycle, with a fake contentWindow (EJS_emulator stub): - a 'ready' from another window is ignored - the iframe's 'ready' registers - restartNonce remount and exit dispose - missing internals mark clips unavailable with a reason code and no throw - the tap attaches to AL.currentCtx.gain 6) Privacy: a blob: ROM URL yields title null, and the end card and file name show only the console. 7) The idle predicate follows paused, settingsMenuOpen and disksMenuOpen. Clip layout

Portrait 720x1280 default (phone share), landscape 1280x720 alt; both re-rendered from the log (no crop), so the editor toggle is instant. Portrait: full bg #faf8ef (Game.tsx:378), brand bar 0-96, HUD boxes centered at y~120-220, board 648x648 at x=36,y=280 (#bbada0 frame), bottom brand 1180-1280. Smooth vector drawing (no pixelation); text in the body font (live probe on prod: computed family 'Nunito, "Nunito Fallback"'), loaded via document.fonts.load('700 36px Nunito') before first frame. 30 fps.

Brag events

All detection is a pure brags(entry, round) in clip/brags.ts, evaluated by the kit on each append; dedupe key per round. (a) newHighScore: move entry where key.score > round.bestAtStart AND previous entry score <= bestAtStart AND bestAtStart > 0 (first-ever game stays silent, matching Trophy Case evaluate.ts:114-121); key `nhs:<round>` so undo (store.ts:192-210 lowers score but never state.highScore) and a re-cross cannot re-fire. Never compare against state.highScore at event time: move() already raised it (:127). Pre 8 s / post 3 s. (b) win: move entry with key.status==='won' and prev status!=='won' (store.ts:140-143); key `win:<round>` (undo after a win re-enters :140 today, see fixes). Pre 15 s / post 4 s, slow-mo 0.5x on the 2048 merge (1.2 s). (c) bigTile: first time this round a tile of 512/1024/4096/8192 appears in key.grid AND value > round.highestTileAtStart; key `tile:<v>:<round>`. (d) gameOverRecap: move entry with status 'game-over' (store.ts:145-147) and score > bestAtStart -> last 20 s + result card. Auto-clips fire in all modes (single player).

Audio

Current sites: none. games/2048 has no AudioContext/Audio/oscillator; the only route audio is shared read-aloud speechSynthesis (shared/hooks/useReadAloud.ts:34,111), which must stay out of clips. Add clip/sfx.ts: `sfx(entry) => SfxCue[]`; the kit plays cues live on the shared bus when the entry is appended and re-synthesizes the same cues in an OfflineAudioContext for the clip (so sound follows events, one source of truth, and idle-compression retiming stays in sync). Cues (all synthesized, no assets, variation seeded by entry.seq): move -> 'slide' 60 ms band-passed noise swish (1.2 kHz, gain .15); merge -> one 'pop' per move at the largest merged value, f = 330*1.12^(log2(v)-1) Hz sine+triangle 90 ms (4 -> 370 Hz, 2048 -> ~1025 Hz); spawn -> soft 20 ms tick; win -> C5-E5-G5-C6 arpeggio 90 ms steps; game over -> G4-E4-C4 triangle descend; undo -> reversed swish; newHighScore crossing -> 2-ping sparkle (1.3/1.9 kHz). Gated by the bus's persisted global mute (2048 has no per-game soundEnabled field; do not add one unless the shared shell mute is not shipped).

Replay log and renderer

Doc: {v:1, app:'2048', round, meta:{bestAtStart, highestTileAtStart, resumed}, entries:[{seq, t, type:'move'|'undo'|'keep-going'|'round', dir?, from?:Uint8Array(16), slid?:Uint8Array(16), merged?:[r,c][], spawn?:{r,c,v}, gained?, key:{grid:Uint8Array(16) of log2 exponents (0=empty), score, status, keep}}]}. Appended at the store sites above. Clock 'wall' (performance.now) with idle compression gaps >1500 ms -> 600 ms (also removes pause-menu time: 2048/GameShell.tsx passes canPause with no onPause). Size ~40-60 B/move: 60 s ring ~200 moves ~10 KB; a whole 1,000-move game ~50 KB. oregon-trail dom-replay 36 h

Prerequisite fixes

  • The hunting animation loop wipes the herd. Verified on prod (/tmp/hh-clips/g7/input.out): with the mouse still there were three animals, at x = 702, 869 and 91; after ONE mouse move, only a fresh spawn at x = -17 remained. Cause: the effect's deps [cursorPos, recoil, paused] (Hunting.tsx:489) re-arm the loop on every aim move and every shot. 'let lastTime = 0' (269) then makes the first frame's delta timestamp/16, which is in the thousands, so every animal jumps off-screen and is deleted (334-338). On desktop you cannot aim without triggering this.
  • Hunting draws inside setState updaters: animals at 328-389, hit text at 392-414, bullet holes at 417-449. React runs only the first update of a frame eagerly. The others run during a later render task, after the frame has already been presented, and the next frame clears them. Verified: none of 8 screenshots taken 81-196 ms after a miss showed the bullet hole (/tmp/hh-clips/g7/huntfx.out). Rewrite the minigame as one long-lived rAF loop. Keep animals, effects, cursor, timer, score, food and ammo in refs, clamp dt to 50 ms, and draw directly each frame. Push HUD values to React state at 10 Hz or less for the DOM HUD.
  • Two shots per tap on phones. The container has onClick (542) and onTouchStart (543). React's passive touchstart plus the W3C compatibility click run shoot twice (the same class verified on hextris), so each tap costs two bullets. The touchstart path also never moves the crosshair to the tap point. Use one onPointerDown that sets the cursor and fires.
  • Container height. The hunt container is min-h-screen inside the header offset, so the canvas extends 48-56 px below the fold, with the bottom-left legend and the bottom-right hint under it. Measured (/tmp/hh-clips/g7/layout.out): 1440x900 at top 56 on a 900 px desktop viewport, and 750x342 at top 48 in iPhone 13 landscape. Size it to calc(100dvh - header).
  • Hi-DPI backing of min(dpr, 2) for the hunt canvas (resize at 262-265), with setTransform.
  • hunt(food, ammo) (store.ts:119-121) gains a third argument, the per-animal tally, for the end card and the journey log.
  • Add sound effects (the game is silent), written against BaseAudioContext so the recap can reuse them offline.

Registration

Hybrid: a live canvas capture for the Hunting minigame, plus a generated Journey Recap (an offline replay render) for the DOM trail. (a) Hunting. The canvas mounts only in the hunting phase (Game.tsx:49-50) and unmounts when time reaches 0, because Hunting.tsx:491-533 then returns the result screen instead. Register inside Hunting after canvasRef (Hunting.tsx:80): useClipSource({ gameId: 'oregon-trail', sourceId: 'hunt', canvasRef, active: timeLeft > 0, layout: 'auto', fit: 'width', getHud: getHuntHud, paintHud: paintHuntHud, endCard: paintHuntEndCard }). Call clipEvents.runStart('oregon-trail', { segment: 'hunt' }) in a mount effect. Call runEnd when time reaches 0 or on 'End Hunt Early' (618). The shell pause (OregonTrailGameShell.tsx:48-51 -> useHuntPauseStore) must emit clipEvents.phase from setPaused (lib/huntPause.ts:14-16). (b) TravelScene (TravelScene.tsx:600-604) is NOT registered. It is a decorative strip 12-16 rem tall, and a video of the DOM menus cannot be captured on phones and would show the names the kid typed. (c) Journey Recap. The kit's offline composer is started from the victory or game-over screen (GameUI.tsx:20-41). It uses a snapshot of the journey log taken at the phase change (store.ts:108-110), before 'Play Again' calls resetGame (147). The header restart (OregonTrailGameShell.tsx:31-37) restarts the journey, so startGame (69-72) clears the log.

What the compositor paints

Hunting: the canvas has the scene, the animals (emoji fillText, Hunting.tsx:383), the hit text and the crosshair. The timer, score, ammo and food (549-577) are DOM, held in component-local useState (100-108). After the prerequisite rewrite the loop keeps them in refs, and getHuntHud returns { timeLeft, score, food, carryMax: MAX_CARRY_WEIGHT 200, ammoLeft, lowAmmo }. paintHuntHud mirrors the DOM HUD: - Top-left: a 'Time {t}s' card (text-red-400 #f87171, pulsing at 10 s or less). - Top-center: 'Score {score}' (amber-400 #fbbf24). - Top-right: '🎯 {ammo}' and '🍖 {food}/200'. - Cards use rgba(0,0,0,0.5) with radius 8. Skip the animal legend and the controls hint. paintHuntEndCard: 3 s over the held last frame, dimmed. It shows 'Hunt Complete!' (amber-200 #fde68a on green-700 #15803d at 90%), '🍖 {food} lbs of meat', '🎯 {ammoUsed} bullets used', 'Score {score}', '🎒 Carrying capacity reached!' when food >= 200, and a per-animal tally row ('🦬 x1 🦌 x2 🐰 x3'). No DOM is ever rasterized. leaderName and the party names (TitleScreen.tsx:47, 64; shown at Travel.tsx:144; cloud-synced at store.ts:155-157) must never reach a clip.

Clip layout

Hunt: 'auto' picks the orientation from the source aspect. Landscape phones and desktops give 1280x720; a portrait phone gives 720x1280. Fit-width, smooth scaling. After the container-height prerequisite, the hunt canvas equals the visible area. Recap: rendered natively at the chosen orientation (1280x720 by default, 720x1280 when the kid picks portrait), so nothing is scaled.

Brag events

(1) win, featured. travel() sets gamePhase 'victory' (store.ts:108-110, when result.victory). Emit 'Made it to Oregon!' and offer the recap. (2) A journey that ends in a loss (game_over, same line) gets no brag mark. Offer the recap as 'My trail story' instead. (3) Hunt. At time 0 or on End Hunt Early, the mark is featured when food >= 100 or food reached MAX_CARRY_WEIGHT ('{food} lbs of food!'), and standard otherwise. Each buffalo or deer hit (in shoot, Hunting.tsx:214-234, where config.meat >= 60) gets a standard mark, for example 'Got a buffalo!'. Three hits in a row with no miss emit 'Sharpshooter!'. (4) Recap beats, not video clips. Each landmark reached (store.ts:103-107), each river crossing with its method and result (crossRiver 123-141), and each severe event (events.ts:19-23) is appended to the journey log and becomes a signpost beat in the recap. This game has no persisted best score, so there is no newHighScore event.

Audio

Silent today: games/oregon-trail has no AudioContext, Audio element or oscillator. Add lib/sounds.ts on the shared bus, gated by the shared sound preference. Write the functions against BaseAudioContext plus a destination node, so the SAME functions play live and also render into the recap's OfflineAudioContext. Hunting sounds: - Shot: a cartoon cork 'pop' (25 ms of noise through a 1.2 kHz bandpass plus a 180 Hz blip), in the shoot handler. Never a realistic gunshot (kid-safe, Guardrail 1). - Hit: a 'boing' (sine 300->600 Hz, 150 ms) plus a soft thud (214-234). - Miss: a dirt 'puff' (lowpassed noise, 80 ms) (242-247). - Countdown ticks at 5..1 s, a hunt-complete fanfare, and a carry-cap chime. Trail sounds (live, and reused in the recap): a positive-event chime, a negative 'bonk', a low stinger for severe events, a river splash (noise sweep), a landmark bell, and a victory fanfare. Recap music bed: a 4-chord plucked loop at 100 bpm (Karplus-Strong or short triangle plucks), rendered offline.

Replay log and renderer

Journey log: new, in lib/journeyLog.ts, appended by the store actions, and never containing names. Shape: { v: 1, month, occupation, startedAt, days: DayRow[], events: Beat[], final?: { victory, score, reason, day, miles, alive, partySize } }. - DayRow is a compact tuple [day, miles, weatherIdx, paceIdx, food, alive, sick]. travel() (store.ts:89-111) appends one. rest() (113-117) appends one too, with miles unchanged and a rest-marker Beat. - Beat is [day, kind, idx, extra]. Kinds: - 'event': an index into ALL_EVENTS (events.ts:30), appended where travel() sets currentEvent (102). - 'landmark': a LANDMARKS index (103-107). - 'river': the river index, depth, method and success, from crossRiver (123-141). - 'hunt': food, ammoUsed and a per-animal tally, from Hunting's end state through hunt(food, ammo, tally) (Hunting.tsx:525 and 615). - 'store': from leaveStore (88). Size: checkGameOver caps the journey at day 200 (gameLogic.ts:63). That is at most 200 DayRows of about 20 bytes each plus at most 60 beats, about 5-6 KB of JSON. Storage: its own persisted key, 'oregon-trail-journey'. It is NOT part of getProgress/setProgress (store.ts:150-200), so it never goes to the server and needs no schema change. A journey resumed from the cloud on another device starts its log on the resume day, and the recap says 'from Day N'. Renderer: games/oregon-trail/lib/recap.ts, driven by the kit's offline composer with explicit frame timestamps at 30 fps, 14-22 s long. 1. First, extract TravelScene's drawing into a pure lib/travelRender.ts: drawTravel(ctx, w, h, { day, weather, pace, scroll, t }). This covers TIME_PALETTES (TravelScene.tsx:8-33), the sky, clouds, mountains, hills, ground, dust, oxen, wagon and weather functions, and the parallax update (468-583). The live TravelScene and the recap both use it, so the recap is pixel-identical to what the kid saw. 2. Title card, 1.5 s: 'The Oregon Trail', the handle, and 'Left Independence in {Month}'. 3. Trail, 10-16 s: the wagon scene, with the day, weather and pace from each DayRow, at about 12 days per second. A trail bar across the bottom mirrors ProgressMap (ProgressMap.tsx:23-107): an amber-800/50 track, an amber-600->500 fill, landmark dots that turn green-500 when passed, a blue ring for rivers, and the 🚗 marker, with 'Day {d}' and '{miles} / 2000 miles'. 4. Each Beat pops a 0.9 s signpost card showing the event title only, for example 'Broken Wheel!'. Cards use Event.tsx:7's colors: green-700 for positive, red-700 for severe, amber-700 otherwise. Rivers show 'Kansas River: forded!'; hunts show '🎯 +{food} lbs'. 5. Finale, 3 s: 'You made it to Oregon!' with 'Day {d} · Score {calculateScore}' (gameLogic.ts:66-70) and '{alive} of {partySize} made it'. Or, on a loss: 'The trail ended on Day {d}: {reason}'. Party members appear only as a count, never by name.

Tests

journeyLog tests: - travel() appends one DayRow per day and a Beat for each event, landmark and river. - The log never contains leaderName or any party name: serialize it and assert that none of the names passed to startGame appear. - resetGame clears the log. - A synthetic 200-day journey is under 8 KB. travelRender tests: drawTravel is pure (the same inputs give the same recorded draw calls), and TravelScene uses it. recap tests (recording context, fixed log): the timeline has the title, one signpost per Beat, and the finale text, and no draw call contains a party name. Hunting rewrite tests: - With rAF stubbed and a scripted clock, a mousemove does not remove on-screen animals. This is the regression test for the wipe. - One pointerdown uses exactly one bullet. - The HUD ref values update. - The end card lists the per-animal tally. - getHuntHud returns ammoLeft = supplies.ammunition - ammoUsed.

Group notes from the spec writers

Canvas group A

GROUP: arkanoid, asteroids, blitz-bomber, bomberman, breakout, dino-runner, endless-runner. All seven games are single Canvas2D surfaces that are always mounted. There are no second canvases, no key-remounts, no WebGL and no DOM replay, so preserveDrawingBuffer and replay do not apply. Two games (arkanoid, bomberman) keep their HUD and result screens in the DOM and need kit-painted overlays. The other five paint the HUD and result cards into the canvas. VERIFIED LIVE ON hankshits.com, 2026-09-27 (Playwright, canvas-call instrumentation, rAF 121/s on a 120 Hz Mac): (1) Blitz-bomber, dino-runner and endless-runner update only on every other frame, at half the designed speed: - Blitz: plane on 60 of 121 frames, 89 px/s against a designed 180. - Dino: score 3 in 0.9 s against about 5.4. - Endless: 15.5 m/s against about 30. - Motion steps at half the display rate, so any clip judders. (2) Bomberman keyboard hold-to-move is dead. The player stayed on tile (1,1) through 0.7-1.2 s holds of ArrowRight and ArrowDown while playing. Root cause, one sentence: the game loops are React effects whose dependencies change on every store update, so the rAF loop is torn down every frame and loses its timers (the dt skip-frame reset, moveTimer). Class fix, required for this group: shared/hooks/useGameLoop(cb, { running, fixedStepMs?, maxDtMs = 50, timeScale }). It has one rAF chain per mount, the callback in a ref, state read through getState(), and a dt clamp. It exposes onAfterRender, so the clip kit captures exactly post-render. - Frame-stepped simulations (asteroids, breakout) use fixedStepMs 1000/60. This also fixes today's 2x speed on 120 Hz screens. - Blitz, dino and endless keep today's feel with timeScale 0.5. Jack decides whether to restore the designed 1.0; this is a gameplay feel choice, not the spec's. Not yet filed as issues. Bomberman's keyboard is a real playability bug for desktop kids. WHAT THE SHARED KIT MUST SUPPORT FOR THIS GROUP: (a) useClipSource(appId, cfg). It keeps cfg in a ref and registers once per canvas element. All seven components re-render every frame because they subscribe with no selector, so registration must never churn. It reads canvas() on every tick and tolerates source-size changes (arkanoid rotation) by re-fitting and inserting a cut. It needs: - phase(): 'menu' | 'playing' | 'paused' | 'interstitial' | 'result'. The store is the only reliable pause signal: asteroids and bomberman have in-game pause buttons that bypass GameShell, and dino and endless have canPause=false. - hud() for the title, end and story cards. - Optional paintHud/paintResult. These read store state only and never rasterize the DOM. - A layout with gameRect and band colors, and an fps hint. (b) Result-freeze. Keep sampling while phase==='result' or 'interstitial'. If the phase leaves before a 2.5 s post-roll ends (instant restart), finish the post-roll from the last result frame, copied into a freeze canvas with drawImage every tick. For games whose loop stops outside 'playing' (arkanoid, asteroids), repeat the last frame. (c) A pause-on-document.hidden rule plus a cut marker, for canPause=false games. (d) A non-React emitter, clipEvents.emit(appId, event), callable from zustand stores and a no-op when no kit is mounted. Also a createRunBrag helper with start(prevBest), score(n) (newHighScore fires once per run on the first crossing of bestAtRunStart, and only when bestAtRunStart > 0), tier(n) (once per tier per run), end(score) (strict greater-than, returns isNewBest) and raiseBest(n) (a mid-run cloud sync raises the snapshot). Why the snapshot rule matters: breakout (store.ts:828) and blitz (store.ts:362) bump progress.highScore mid-run, and dino and bomberman compute the flag after maxing. So 'compare against the previous best' only works against the snapshot. Event weights: 'highlight' auto-saves, 'marker' only chapters the ring. Types: runStart, runAbort, cut, newHighScore, levelClear, win, combo, milestone, bonus, runEnd. The achievements poll (useAuthSync.ts:424-436, evaluate.ts:115-121) is a lagging fallback for kid-made games only. It is up to 1 s late and silent on first scores. (e) shared/lib/gameAudio: - One AudioContext, per-app input gains, a per-app speaker mute (setSpeakerMuted), a master DynamicsCompressor and limiter, and a record tap taken before the speaker gain. Clips keep game sound when a kid mutes the speakers, and the editor has its own 'Game sounds' switch. - voice(appId, key, {maxPerSec}) caps voices. Arkanoid chains, breakout multi-ball and bomberman bomb chains fire dozens of oscillators per frame. - startLoop/stopLoop handles sustained voices (asteroids thrust and UFO, blitz engine) and auto-stops them on pause and unmount. - A capture-phase pointerdown/keydown resume in GameShell. WebKit documents Web Audio as using sticky activation (webkit.org/blog/13862/the-user-activation-api), but a central resume also covers older iOS and contexts first created inside rAF, which is what asteroids, bomberman and breakout do today. - Keep the default 'ambient' session. Do NOT set navigator.audioSession.type='playback': it bypasses the ringer switch (bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=237322; MDN AudioSession/type, Safari 16.4). Device-test whether the record tap still receives samples with the ringer switch on. - Every SFX in this group is mono oscillator or noise synthesis, so mono capture loses nothing. (f) Codec and sizes: - Use H.264 Level 4.0 (for example avc1.640028). Level 3.1 caps 1280x720 at 30 fps (MaxFS 3,600 MBs, 108,000 MB/s), and 720p60 needs Level 3.2 or higher (en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advanced_Video_Coding, levels table). - Output sizes stay at or under 921,600 px, with even sides and multiples of 16 where possible: asteroids 960x960, blitz 1024x768, bomberman 936x900, breakout 720x960, dino 1280x720, endless 1280x720, arkanoid 720x1280 or 1280x720. - The six fixed-size games get a shared useCanvasBackingScale plus ctx.setTransform(S,...) at the start of render, so the compositor draws 1:1 with no resampling. Crisp HUD text also sharpens today's CSS-upscaled on-screen canvases on retina phones. - Nothing in these games reads canvas.width except arkanoid (grep verified), and arkanoid already renders at DPR. None of these games use bitmap sprites, so nearest-neighbour upscaling is not the right tool here. Keep imageSmoothingEnabled=false only for future pixel-sprite games that declare pixelArt:true. - Adaptive fps: 60 for fast games, 30 for bomberman, and a drop to 30 when the encoder queue grows. (g) The result share chip must be DOM, outside the canvas click path, with stopPropagation. In five games a canvas tap or Space restarts. Put the chip above z-50 (bomberman's fixed modals) and below the GameShell header (z-1000). It must also clear the IOSInstallPrompt bottom sheet, using the useStartOverlayPresence pattern from lessons.md. Dino and endless have no pause menu, so the 'Clip it!' entry cannot live only in PauseMenu. (h) A shared result-input lockout helper: ignore e.repeat and use a 600 ms tap/key lockout after runEnd or an interstitial. Every game in the group needs it. CROSS-CUTTING PERF, needed for recording headroom on low-end phones: - Zustand persist 5.0.14 writes storage on EVERY set() (apps/web/node_modules/zustand/esm/middleware.mjs:358-373). All seven stores call set() at least once per frame, which is 60-120 synchronous localStorage writes per second. - Fix the class with a deduped storage wrapper in shared/lib (cache the last serialized string per key), and stop the per-frame lastModified bumps in bomberman (store.ts:795) and breakout (store.ts:869). - Narrow the whole-store subscriptions where the kit bench shows frame time over 16.7 ms p95 while recording. TESTS, KIT LEVEL: - A shared src/__tests__/canvas2d-mock.ts (a recording 2D-context mock). jsdom returns null for '2d', and setup.ts only mocks WebGL. - A fake-rAF driver for useGameLoop tests. - A Playwright clip smoke per game against a local build: MP4 dims, fps, non-blank frames, non-zero audio RMS, ends on the result frame. Run it on a quiet machine, per lessons.md. FILE AS ISSUES (pre-existing, not clip-blocking): - Breakout power-up timers use wall-clock time through pause (store.ts:462-474, :747-761). - Bomberman shows two pause UIs at once (the GameShell menu over the game's own PAUSED modal, Game.tsx:393-409). - Blitz counts gamesPlayed on every landing (store.ts:366). - Bomberman keyboard: fixed by this spec, but file it so it is tracked. EFFORT: about 98.5 expert hours for the seven per-game integrations. That covers the prerequisite fixes, new SFX, sound controls and tests. It excludes the shared kit, useGameLoop (about 4 h), useCanvasBackingScale (about 1 h), createRunBrag (about 2 h) and the gameAudio bus, which belong to the kit budget. MAKE-A-GAME SKILL: once the kit ships, the template should wire useGameLoop, useClipSource, createRunBrag and gameAudio by default, so kid-built games get clips without per-game work.

Canvas group B

GROUP 7: flappy-bird, hextris, hill-climb, math-attack, oregon-trail, platformer, space-invaders. All seven draw with Canvas2D; none uses WebGL, R3F or an iframe. Oregon Trail is the only hybrid: a live canvas hunt plus an offline journey recap. Total expert effort is about 124.5 h on top of the shared kit. 1) EVIDENCE FROM THIS PASS All tests ran against prod https://hankshits.com, Playwright 1.62 Chromium, read-only play. Scripts and raw outputs are in /tmp/hh-clips/g7/. - Loops (verify-loops.js -> loops.out): the loop re-arms every frame, so the simulation steps every other frame. - flappy: 57.4 cancelAnimationFrame calls/s at 114.8 fps. - platformer: 57.9 at 116.6. - space-invaders: 59.9 at 119.7. - Consequence: platformer Level 1 took 11,534 ms of wall time, but the stored bestTime was 5,862 ms. The platformer timer runs at half speed. - Space Invaders AudioContext: 'suspended' at load, 'running' only after the first gesture. - Input (verify-input.js -> input.out): - Oregon hunt: 3 on-screen animals were gone after ONE mouse move. - Hextris: one phone tap = 2 rotations (targetRotation 2.094 rad). - Space Invaders controls (verify-si.js -> si.out): the Move-left DOM node is replaced within 100 ms, and a 1 s hold moved the cannon 5 px. - Also from si.out: during play, the iOS 'Play Fullscreen!' prompt covered the Space Invaders Move-left button in iPhone emulation (hit-test landed on the prompt). IOSInstallPrompt.tsx:48-53 hides only while the start card shows, so it slides up, fixed at the bottom with z-[200], the moment play starts. - Layout (verify-layout.js -> layout.out): the hill-climb and hunt canvases are innerWidth x innerHeight starting at y=56/48, so 56/48 px sit below the fold. The backing store is 1x even at DPR 3. - Hunt effects (verify-hunt-fx.js -> huntfx.out): none of 8 screenshots showed the hunt's bullet hole. 2) WHAT THE SHARED KIT MUST SUPPORT FOR THIS GROUP (a) useClipSource options: - { gameId, sourceId?, canvasRef, active?, layout: 'portrait' | 'landscape' | 'auto', fit?: 'contain' | 'width', pixelated?, getHud, paintHud, endCard? }. - canvasRef.current is re-read on every tick. Math Attack mounts a new canvas every run; hill-climb remounts through a key change and through the Garage. - A null or inactive source holds the last frame. - Registrations are keyed by gameId, so a remount continues the same ring buffer. (b) Data and painter contracts: - getHud() returns plain, serializable data and is sampled once per compositor frame. - paintHud(ctx, hud, rects) and endCard(ctx, info, rects, tMs) are pure functions. - rects = { frame, game, top, bottom, orientation }. - Store the HUD snapshots as a side track, so export can re-lay the overlay for portrait or landscape. (c) Module-level clipEvents, importable from zustand stores (no React, no-op in SSR and jsdom). This mirrors the Steam Timeline model of game modes plus prioritized timed events (https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/features/timeline): - runStart, runEnd, phase('playing' | 'paused' | 'menu'). - mark({ kind, title, detail?, priority: 'standard' | 'featured', preMs?, postMs?, dedupe? }). (d) Pause must be driven by store phase, not by the shell's isPaused. Hextris's in-game 'II' button and Space Invaders' mobile PAUSE button call store.pauseGame directly, and hill-climb and math-attack disable the shell pause. (e) A post-roll ends at the next runStart. There is a minimum end-card hold: when a restart cuts the post-roll, freeze-extend the last frame to at least 1.5 s. (f) A shared hi-DPI helper (backing = logical x min(dpr, 2), plus a per-frame setTransform). None of the 7 canvases uses DPR today. (g) A shared audio bus in shared/audio: - getGameAudio() returns { ctx, input } or null. - Master chain: a DynamicsCompressor limiter, then master gain, which feeds both the destination and the recorder tap. - resume() on the first pointerdown/keydown capture listener. The MDN Autoplay guide says contexts created without a gesture start suspended; WebKit gives no auto-resume. - sfx helpers (tone, noise, arp, loop) that accept any BaseAudioContext plus a destination, so the Oregon recap and the DOM-replay games can render the same sounds with OfflineAudioContext. - A global sound preference with a shared SoundToggle on GameStartOverlay and PauseMenu. Flappy, Platformer and Oregon have no settings UI; per-game soundEnabled gates stay in hextris, space-invaders, hill-climb and math-attack. (h) An offline composition mode, used for the Oregon recap: explicit-timestamp frames plus OfflineAudioContext audio. (i) Canvas text in the bands must use the loaded font. next/font renames Nunito, so read getComputedStyle(document.body).fontFamily and await document.fonts.ready. (j) Clip UI placement: - The header is full. - Hill-climb's window touch zones claim every touch unless the target is filtered. - The iOS install prompt currently slides up, fixed at the bottom, the moment play starts. Restrict it to non-play moments, or the clip bar and the game controls are covered. 3) CLASS DEFECTS FOUND (fix the class, not the instance) (i) React registers touchstart as passive (react-dom-client.development.js:19251-19262 in the repo's React 19.2.3), so onTouchStart plus onClick double-fires through the W3C Touch Events §9 compatibility click (https://w3c.github.io/touch-events/). Instances: hextris Game.tsx:427-428 (verified), space-invaders Game.tsx:998-1004, oregon Hunting.tsx:542-543. Fix: a single onPointerDown, the flappy/platformer precedent. (ii) Components defined inside components are remounted every render (https://react.dev/learn/preserving-and-resetting-state). Instances: space-invaders Game.tsx:755, 899, 948 (verified). (iii) rAF effects keyed on per-render callbacks that reset lastTime: - Oregon hunt: catastrophic, wipes the animals (verified). - platformer: half-speed timer (verified). - flappy and space-invaders: sim at half the frame rate. Harmless for clips because the canvas repaints every frame. (iv) Movement per update instead of per dt, which ties difficulty to the display refresh rate: hextris store.ts:369-370 and 495-498; space-invaders store.ts:384, 394, 469 and 713 (bullets, alien fire chance, UFO). On 120 Hz Android these games run twice as hard. (v) No DPR on any canvas. (vi) New-best checks made after the write are true on a tie: hill-climb GameOverScreen.tsx:28, math-attack Game.tsx:380, space-invaders Game.tsx:534. Standard fix: a pre-write flag plus a bestAtRunStart snapshot, maxed with mid-run cloud setProgress. 4) PRE-EXISTING, NOT CLIP-RELATED: file as issues with evidence (PR-scope carve-out) - Space Invaders keyboard movement and auto-fire run per loop frame (Game.tsx:634-648), so the cannon is 2x faster on 120/144 Hz monitors. This is the re-arm class plus (iv). - Platformer totalStars adds starsThisRun on every completion (store.ts:607), and the leaderboard ranks by totalStars (leaderboard-extractors.ts:282-292). Replaying level 1 farms leaderboard score. - The iOS install prompt covers bottom controls during play in every game with bottom controls (shared IOSInstallPrompt.tsx:48-53). 5) PRIVACY RULES FOR OVERLAYS - Never rasterize DOM. - Show only the leaderboard handle. - Never paint age-named difficulty labels (math-attack DIFFICULTY_SETTINGS labels 'N years old', space-invaders age picker). Use speed stars. - Oregon: no leaderName or party names anywhere, including the journey log. Party members appear only as a count. - Keep the Oregon hunting audio cartoony (cork pop, boing). 6) SOURCES - The file:line reads above (repo HEAD 20d56b1). - W3C Touch Events: https://w3c.github.io/touch-events/ - react.dev Preserving and Resetting State: https://react.dev/learn/preserving-and-resetting-state - MDN Autoplay guide: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Media/Guides/Autoplay - MDN Web Audio best practices: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Web_Audio_API/Best_practices - Steam Timelines: https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/features/timeline - Measurement scripts and outputs: /tmp/hh-clips/g7/

3D and iframe group

These notes cover the whole group (monster-truck, four-wheeler-3d, four-wheeler-adventure, retro-arcade). They list what the shared kit (apps/web/src/shared/clips) MUST support. Every item was checked against source code or a primary spec on 2026-09-27. 1. Sources push frames; the kit does not pull them. - None of these four games can be captured from the kit's own rAF. - R3F games push from addAfterEffect, which runs after all roots render in the same task (R3F 9.6.1 events-b389eeca.esm.js:16071-16100). useFrame runs before the render. - EmulatorJS pushes from a microtask queued by the first default-framebuffer draw in a task. - The adventure pushes from the iframe's own rAF. - The HTML "update the rendering" step runs a child document's rAF callbacks after its container's (html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html). A parent-rAF read of the emulator's preserveDrawingBuffer:false canvas therefore returns the cleared buffer, which is black (WebGL 1.0 spec section 2.2: the buffer is cleared after compositing and only re-presented after a draw). - Contract: source.pushFrame(ts, draw), where draw() runs synchronously inside that call and paints the source into the compositor. The kit decimates to the source fps and holds the last good frame. 2. iframe realms have their own clocks. Convert every iframe timestamp (rAF ts, performance.now, getOutputTimestamp().performanceTime) with (iframeWin.performance.timeOrigin - performance.timeOrigin). Each document has its own time origin (w3c.github.io/hr-time). Without this, A/V sync and brag timestamps drift by the iframe's load offset. 3. Output size is fixed for the whole session; the source rect may change. - Games change size mid-session: canvas resize at adventure index.html:668, DPR changes, rotation, emulator hi-res modes. - Remounts happen: keyed restarts in all four games, and a new iframe per emulator session. - The compositor's output stays fixed. It recomputes the source rect each frame and hot-swaps sources, forcing a keyframe without resetting the ring. An encoder restart per resize is the wrong design. - Fit rule 'auto': cover within ±20% of 16:9, otherwise contain, with the bars used as the HUD strip. - The R3F games need cropTop:'shell-header', because their canvases are fixed inset-0 under the 48/56px header. - Per-source fps and bitrate hints are needed: 30 fps / 4 Mbps for 3D, 30 / 3 for the 2D iframe, 60 / 3 for retro. - Pixel-art sources need a nearest-neighbor path plus a "native recovery" intermediate canvas. 4. idle() predicate per source. The kit stops appending video and audio while idle and collapses the gap. Gaps are start screens, pause menus, menu panels (except four-wheeler-3d's 'fishing'), EJS menus, and document.hidden. - The shared overlays that cover a game should publish presence the way shared/lib/startOverlayPresence.ts does: OrientationWarning, RestartConfirmationDialog, LeaderboardModal and IOSInstallPrompt. The kit idles while any is up. - R3F games render every frame even while paused, so idle() is the only thing that keeps pause screens out of replays. 5. HUD and brag UI are painted by the kit, never rasterized from the DOM. - Use a declarative HUD model: chips (icon, text, color), an optional gauge (value, max, redline), an optional race panel and countdown, and an escape-hatch paintExtra(ctx, frameRect, scale) for things like copying the adventure's #speedo canvas. - Paint from getState() values with memoized strings, so no per-frame allocations. - Banners are driven by brag events, and there are end-card and result-card templates. - Emoji in fillText depend on OS fonts; that is fine on iOS, Android, ChromeOS and macOS. - The kit's own UI must follow the no-slop rules: no gradients, glows or colored edge stripes. 6. Brag events. - Module-level clips.emit({appId, type, priority, title, payload}), callable from useFrame and store actions without React. - Priority 2 or higher auto-saves a highlight: pre-roll 8s, post-roll 2s, plus a result card when the game freezes on the moment (four-wheeler-3d race finish). - Priority 1 drops a replay marker only. The kit coalesces events within 1s. - Rising-edge detection stays in the game and must compare against the value read BEFORE the write. Never subscribe to persisted fields: cloud hydration (useAuthSync -> setProgress) and persist rehydrate flip them too (monster-truck challenges). - iframe games baseline on the first probe after every load. 7. Audio. - The class defect: no game here has a tappable output. monster-truck has 11 ctx.destination sites. four-wheeler-3d has 4 AudioContexts and closes 3 of them. The adventure's one-shots bypass its master. EJS uses its own context. - Shared in-page context: getGameAudio(){ctx, channel(name), unlock()}, never closed, with a master gain feeding destination plus an AudioWorklet tap. - unlock() must run synchronously inside a gesture handler: a document-level capture listener for pointerdown, touchend and keydown, plus a call in GameStartOverlay's click. It must not run from a React effect. monster-truck's current first-click resume fires before its context exists, then removes itself (Game.tsx:136-149). - Foreign-realm taps: attachForeignTap(node, realmWin) installs the SAME worklet in another realm's context. Construct it with realmWin.AudioWorkletNode, transfer the port to the encoder worker, and map clocks per item 2. AudioWorklet, AudioWorkletNode.port and getOutputTimestamp are all Safari 14.1+ (mdn/browser-compat-data api/*.json). Zero-fill whenever a tap is suspended, silent or dead (its realm unloaded). - CSP: an AudioWorklet module fetch is governed by script-src-elem, falling back to script-src (w3.org/TR/CSP3, "Get the effective directive": audioworklet -> script-src-elem). The main-route script-src (next.config.ts:35) has no blob:, so the worklet MUST be a same-origin file, e.g. new URL('./tap.worklet.js', import.meta.url), never a blob URL. srcdoc iframes inherit the parent CSP; /emulator has its own CSP, which allows 'self' and blob:. - Add a class-level guard test: no 'new AudioContext', 'webkitAudioContext' or '.destination' under src/games/** except through the kit. Static iframe games (public/games/**) are covered by preludes. 8. iframe helpers the kit should own. - injectPrelude(html, script), which puts the script as the first child of head and fails loudly otherwise. - Realm disposal on iframe swap. - restoreFocus after any kit UI closes. Both iframe games lose keyboard control when a header button takes focus. - The kit's keyboard shortcut must avoid every game's bindings. The adventure alone uses WASD, arrows, Space, B, C, E, F, G, M, N, O, P, R and T. iframe games forward the key from their prelude. - Invariant tests that neither game iframe gains a sandbox attribute. 9. Shell placement. All four games pass canPause={false} (monster-truck/GameShell.tsx:22, four-wheeler-3d/GameShell.tsx:29, the adventure page.tsx:36, retro page.tsx:30). The shared PauseMenu never mounts, so the Clip control must live in the header right cluster. It must never go before the title, because four-wheeler-3d/adventure.css:917 targets nth-child(2). The retro route lacks appId. 10. Memory and performance on phones. - Encode from a ≤1280x720 compositor, never the DPR canvas. The spike measured Monster Truck at 41 fps idle, 4-6 fps recording the raw canvas, and about 34 fps through a 960-wide compositor. - Default the iOS ring to 30s for the two 3D games. Four-wheeler-3d is the heaviest scene. - Adapt fps down (30 to 24) when the game's own rAF interval degrades. 11. Pre-existing defects found while reading. Classify them per the PR-scope rule; items (c) and (e) are one-liners. (a) monster-truck flip detector wraps at ±π and credits false flips (Vehicle.tsx:138-139). (b) monster-truck triple horn that ignores soundEnabled (Game.tsx:124-126, MobileControls.tsx:86-91, Vehicle.tsx:316-327). Merges with #18i. (c) monster-truck start hint says Shift boosts, but Shift brakes (Game.tsx:232 vs useControls.ts:84-90). (d) monster-truck pause does not pause physics (Game.tsx:74). (e) monster-truck dead music toggle, and an engine sound that is never started. (f) Likely silent on iOS: monster-truck, whose context is created outside a gesture and never resumed, and the adventure, which never calls resume(). Both need real-iPhone confirmation. (g) retro EmulatorView's Exit bar is hidden under the GameShell header (Game.tsx:274-295). (h) retro 'ready' accepts any same-origin window (Game.tsx:211). (i) four-wheeler-3d Photos saves via <a download> of a data: URL (PhonePanel.tsx:861), which lands in Files, not Photos, on iOS. 12. Owner decisions (my proposals, not settled policy): whether retro-arcade clips may use the hosted /c/ link path (issue #25i gory titles, plus commercial-ROM footage on our domain), and nothing else. Hunting in four-wheeler-3d and the adventure is existing game content that matches Hank's stated interests; I add no restriction there. I deliberately exclude the adventure's casino wins from auto-clips as a design choice; flag it if the owner disagrees. 13. QA matrix for this group: - real iPhone on iOS 26 (AudioEncoder present) and iOS 17/18 (fallback audio path) - a mid-range Android on Chrome - a school Chromebook - desktop at 60 Hz and 120 Hz; the 120 Hz case exercises the EJS decimation and dirty gate Each run records a 30s instant replay per game and checks decode, the frame-luma floor, HUD presence and audio RMS. 14. Effort totals for this group (expert hours, on top of the shared kit): monster-truck 41, four-wheeler-3d 32, four-wheeler-adventure 34, retro-arcade 26. That is about 133h. It is dominated by the audio-bus migrations, the sound effects that were never there (engine, music, celebrations), and truthful brag detection (the flip wrap fix and persisted records).

DOM-replay group

GROUP VERDICT: all 8 are pure React DOM (re-verified in code; live prod probe at 390x844 DPR3 found no canvas). No phone API can capture DOM pixels, so every one is a `dom-replay` source: the game appends a tiny event log, and the clip is RE-RENDERED into a canvas and encoded after the fact. That beats a live compositor for this group on every axis. Steady-state cost while playing is ~0 (no painting or encoding, so no fps or battery hit on Chromebooks). Frames are exact. Portrait and landscape are re-rendered, not cropped, so the editor toggle, trim, slow-mo and idle-compression are instant and cost nothing until export. My Clips can store replay docs of a few KB instead of MP4s. SHARED KIT MUST SUPPORT (apps/web/src/shared/clips/replay): 1) createReplayLog<S,E>({appId, clock:'tick'|'game'|'wall', keyframeEvery:'event'|'round'|{ms}|{ticks}, reduce?, ringMs(default 90000)}) with round(key, meta) / append(ev, keyAfter?) / slice(from,to) (always self-contained: starts at a keyframe) / subscribe / clearSession. The ring is time-based on the post-retiming timeline and never drops data inside a requested window. Record mode marks a range and streams to OPFS every 10 s (cookie-clicker is the only one that grows past about 100 KB/10 min). 2) useClipReplaySource(source, {active}): registers with the GameShell ClipProvider. It is idempotent under StrictMode, emits a resume keyframe on active false->true, and clears the session on unmount unless a highlight render is pending. Hooks must be called above early returns (memory-match Game.tsx:382). 3) Offline render pipeline: render on the main thread with backpressure-yielding. The DOM paint is 1-3 ms/frame, the fonts and emoji are identical to the page, and it avoids WorkerGlobalScope.fonts plus next/font URL plumbing. Frames come from mediabunny CanvasSource.add(timestamp, duration) with explicit timestamps (faster than real time, promise backpressure: https://mediabunny.dev/guide/media-sources#backpressure). 30 fps by default, 60 fps for snake. The editor preview draws frames live from the log (no encode). The Share button enables only after the File is finalized (transient activation). 4) Timeline retimer: idle-gap compression and slow-mo windows produce a time map applied to both video frames and audio cues. Presets: 'last N s' (applied AFTER compression, so turn-based games get more action), 'whole round', and brag highlight (pre/post roll). 5) Audio for replay sources = cue re-synthesis, not a PCM ring. SFX voices are pure functions (ctx: BaseAudioContext, dest, when, params) that run live on the shared bus and in an OfflineAudioContext (Safari 14.1+, MDN BCD api/OfflineAudioContext). The result goes to mediabunny AudioBufferSource (https://mediabunny.dev/guide/media-sources#audiobuffersource), so mediabunny's AAC path and its WebKit-302253 fix apply. Variation is seeded by entry.seq. One deterministic voice limiter is shared live/offline. There is a persisted global mute, plus per-game soundEnabled gating where the field exists (snake, memory-match, cookie-clicker, wordle all carry DEAD soundEnabled fields today, the same class as issue #24; progress-schemas already declare them, so keep the fields and wire them). Never route speechSynthesis read-aloud into clips. 6) Render kit: portrait 720x1280 / landscape 1280x720 frames, brand bar, handle chip (gaming_profiles.handle only, none for guests), HUD pill primitives, result/title cards, brag banner. - Emoji: an emoji sprite cache rasterized at <=128 px with a blank-pixel check. Skia historically dropped emoji above ~256 px on Android: https://stackoverflow.com/questions/50965784. Desktop Chrome 153 drew 512 px fine in my probe. The sprite also caps memory. - Font: resolve with getComputedStyle(document.body).fontFamily (prod computes 'Nunito, "Nunito Fallback"') plus document.fonts.load per weight before frame 0. - Palette: Tailwind v4 tokens are oklch and partly P3 (node_modules/tailwindcss/theme.css). Ship precomputed sRGB hex via a gamut-mapped converter (e.g. green-800 #016630, amber-600 #da7700, blue-500 #3280ff) instead of parsing oklch in canvas; Chrome parses it but canvas is sRGB and Safari parse behavior is unverified. - Easing library matching CSS ease/ease-out/ease-in-out and Tailwind bounce/pulse/ping. - Avoid ctx.filter (Safari 18+) and letterSpacing (Safari 18.4+); roundRect is fine (Safari 16), per MDN BCD. The VideoFrame baseline is Safari 16.4. 7) Chess pieces: compile react-chessboard's exported defaultPieces (svg/g/path/circle + one matrix transform) to Path2D. This means no image decode, no taint risk, and worker-safe. Plain SVG data URLs do not taint modern Safari either (only iOS 10.3-11.2 did: https://github.com/niklasvh/html2canvas/pull/2683), but Path2D removes the question. 8) Brag pipeline: pure brags(entry, round) per source. Baselines travel in the round meta as primitives, because stores mutate their bests before the UI reads them: 2048 store.ts:127, snake :222, memory :195. Dedupe keys are per round. Auto-highlights run only for single-player or vs-AI play; hot-seat modes (chess local, checkers vs-friend, quoridor local) get manual clips and toasts. 9) Extra share actions per source: Wordle 'Share squares' (text; iOS video share stays files-only) and PNG still cards (cookie-clicker bakery card, wordle squares card). image/png and video/mp4 are both on Chromium's share allowlist (chrome/browser/webshare/share_service_impl.cc:154,166 via https://raw.githubusercontent.com/chromium/chromium/main/chrome/browser/webshare/share_service_impl.cc). 10) Test infrastructure: - RecordingContext2D test double (jsdom has no canvas). - Playwright golden-frame harness: the live board element screenshot vs the renderer output for the same state, compared by SSIM. It needs a test-only store hook (window.__hhClipTest in E2E builds only). - MP4 verification via mediabunny Input. - Seeded Math.random for deterministic runs. 11) Privacy: logs hold only declared game fields and no names. Wordle guesses are dictionary words, and dictionary safety is enforced at the source (see the wordle fixes). Hosted uploads send the MP4, plus optionally the replay doc validated server-side against the same schema. DISCOVERED DEFECTS OUTSIDE CLIP SCOPE (file as issues with this evidence, or fold into the per-game PRs): (a) chess, checkers and quoridor pass canPause with no onPause, so AI moves land under the pause menu (chess store.ts:230,268; checkers :188-229; quoridor :202,264-385). Defer AI while paused. (b) STREAK_ALIASES omits bestWinStreak (shared/lib/achievements/definitions.ts:55-60), so chess, checkers and quoridor never fire streak trophies. This is a one-line fix: do it. (c) checkers picks the FIRST valid move matching a destination (store.ts:154), so two capture chains ending on the same square are ambiguous to the kid. (d) checkers types a 'draw' status that is unreachable, and recordWin treats any non-red status as a black win (store.ts:186). EFFORT: about 177 expert hours across the 8 islands (per-game numbers above: renderer + instrumentation + SFX + brags + fixes + tests + device QA). This excludes the shared kit, which the canvas/R3F/iframe tracks also depend on. Per-game work is 19-27 h, not the feasibility study's 'trivial/small', because every island needs a faithful renderer, real SFX (all 8 are silent today) and the listed correctness fixes. Scratch evidence: /tmp/hh-clips/dom-probe/layout.cjs and layout.out (read-only prod probe), plus the research files /tmp/hh-clips/emoji-canvas.md, svg-taint.md, mediabunny-sources.md, dom-bcd-api-*.json and dom-ssi.cc.

Appendix B: rejected alternatives

AlternativeWhy not
MediaRecorder as the main engineNo portable rolling buffer; Chrome MP4 defaults to VP9/Opus; fragmented output with zero duration. Kept for tiers M and V.
WASM H.264 encoder (minih264, x264, OpenH264 self-build)Distributing an H.264 encoder carries patent exposure (US pool patents until 2027-09-09); x264 is GPL; OpenH264's license covers only Cisco's binaries; phone performance next to a game is unproven. Tier V's native WebM plus the server transcode covers those devices.
ffmpeg.wasm transcoding in the browserAbout 10 MB gzipped, GPL builds, minutes per clip, needs COOP/COEP (breaks the EmulatorJS CDN).
getDisplayMedia / Region or Element CaptureNot on any phone; a permission picker every time on desktop; captures notifications and names.
Per-frame DOM rasterization for DOM gamesTens to hundreds of ms per frame on phones; Safari foreignObject quirks.
Serving clip bytes through the Cloudflare-fronted siteCloudflare's CDN terms on our plan; service egress about $112/month at 10k clips. The only exception is the capped, token-authenticated school fallback on a grey-cloud host, used only after a direct load fails.
Grown-up gate before sharingSuperseded by Jack's settled decision.
Serverless screener with wake-on-grantCold start exceeds the upload time; always-on removes the retry machinery.
Edit-list trimming of video for instant clipsPlayers that ignore edit lists desync or skip video. Instant clips start on a keyframe; the editor smart-cuts. Audio keeps an edit list plus roll groups, bounded to +45 ms in players that ignore them.
Gamer tag or site name on live framesA live capture could leak a name, and packet-copy clips could not remove it.
A single global storage cap for linksOne attacker could turn links off for every kid. Replaced by reserved pools.
A lint rule banning setInterval renderingESLint cannot tell rendering from timers, and existing games use timers correctly. The game-contract test's zero-frames failure catches the real problem.
A fixed 20-minute session timelineAn arbitrary cap. The budget is library headroom, unless the Phase 0 flash-write measurement justifies a stated cap.
A public clip gallery (pending decision 4c)A feed needs heavy moderation and weighs against COPPA and the UK code.
